13
Perilous districts

The purpose of this section is to establish an argument that allows us to say, given a transaction mesh with large enough infrastructure, that the total number of districts in the transaction mesh with interesting behaviour is bounded or there exists a
K_{t}
-minor in our graph.

This manifests in the following statement.

Theorem 13.1
.

Let
t,s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell
be positive integers with
s\geq(2t+24)(8t^{3}+20)+s^{\prime}\ell
and
p\geq(64t^{3}-128)(2t+24)
.
Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with a cylindrical rendition
\rho
in a disk
\Delta
, a nest
\mathcal{C}
of order
s
in
\rho
around the vortex
c_{0}
, and an
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}
.
Further, for each
i\in[\ell+1]
, let
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
be a district of
\mathfrak{M}
, with
G^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell+1}G_{i}
.

Then one of the following holds

i)

the graph
G
contains a
K_{t}
-minor model controlled by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct cycles from
\mathcal{C}
, or

ii)

there exist sets
S\subseteq V(G^{\prime})
and
I=I_{1}\cup I_{2}\subseteq[\ell+1]
with
|S|\leq 8t^{3}-16
,
|I_{1}|\leq 64t^{3}-128
, and
|I_{2}|<\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)
such that each
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell+1]\}
-path in
G^{\prime}-S
is a
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in I_{1}\}
-path and each district
(G_{j}-S,\Omega_{j})
with
j\in[\ell+1]\setminus(I_{1}\cup I_{2})
has a vortex-free rendition in a disk.

Furthermore, there exists an algorithm that, given
\mathfrak{M}
and
\rho
as input, finds one of the outcomes above in time
\mathbf{O}(t^{4}|E(G)|)
.

To analyse the structure we are dealing with, let us first introduce a lemma that allows us to “capture” a bounded number of districts.
We show that we can root a bounded number of districts, together with their nests, on the society of our transaction mesh.
Later, we will reuse the construction from
lemma

5.2
to obtain a large mesh where each of these districts belongs to its private face on the middle row.
These constructions will allow us to directly apply
lemma

4.3
and force a
K_{t}
-minor if too many of the districts we selected were equipped with crosses.

Figure 47
:
An illustration of the outcome of
lemma

13.2
, catching some of the districts in a transaction mesh and connecting their nests down to a part of the outer-most nest.

Lemma 13.2
.

Let
k,q,s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell
be non-negative integers with
1\leq k\leq\ell
,
s\geq(4k+5)q+s^{\prime}\ell
and
p\geq 16kq
.
Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with an
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C}=\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{s}\},\mathfrak{P})
and let
\mathcal{A}=\{(G_{i},\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[k]\}
be a selection
40
40

40

Notice that in the definition of transaction meshes the areas are ordered. We slightly abuse notation here – and many times later on – by assigning new names
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
to the
k
“special” areas we want to deal with.
This is safe because we do not care about the other areas in this process.
of
k
pairwise distinct areas of
\mathfrak{M}
.

Then there exists an extended
q
-surface-wall
D
with signature
(0,0,k)
in
H
such that the base cycles of
D
are the cycles
C_{s-s^{\prime}-q-1},\dots,C_{s-s^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{C}
and there exists a bijection
\phi
between the set
[k]
and the vortex segments
S_{v}
of
D
such that for each
i\in[k]
, the nest of the vortex segment
\phi(i)
is a subset of the district-nest of
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
(See
figure

47
for an illustration.).

Moreover, there exists an algorithm that finds
D
in time
\mathbf{O}(qk|E(G)|)
.

Proof.

By
observation

12.11
each area
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
has a radial linkage
\mathcal{R}_{i}
of order
\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}p\geq 4kq
for the area-nest
\mathcal{C}_{i}=\{C^{i}_{1},\dots,C^{i}_{s-s^{\prime}\ell-1}\}
of
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{R}_{i}
is made up of subpaths of one of the transactions among
\mathfrak{P}
.

For each
i\in[k]
, let
X_{i}^{\prime}
be the set of first vertices of
\mathcal{R}_{i}
we encounter on the cycle
C^{i}_{5q+1}\in\mathcal{C}_{i}
when traversing the paths of
\mathcal{R}_{i}
starting from
C_{1}^{i}
.
Then let
X_{i}\subseteq X_{i}^{\prime}
be an arbitrary selection of
4q
of these vertices.
Moreover, let
X\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in[k]}X_{i}
.

Now let
\mathcal{P}\in\mathfrak{P}
be a transaction of
\mathfrak{M}
such that there exists a subpath
Y^{\prime}
of
C_{s-s^{\prime}}
that contains both endpoints of every member of
\mathcal{P}
.
Notice that such a transaction
\mathcal{P}
must exist by the definition of transaction meshes and our choice of
\ell\geq 1
.
Let
Y
be the set of all endpoints of
\mathcal{P}
on
C_{s-s^{\prime}}
.

It follows from
Menger’s Theorem
that there exists a linkage
\mathcal{L}
of size
|X|=4kq
in
H
between
Y
and
X
, because there are at least
4kq
cycles in
\mathcal{C}
, as well as
4kq
cycles in each
\mathcal{C}_{i}
, at least
4kq
paths from
\mathcal{P}
, and at least
4kq
paths in each
\mathcal{R}_{i}
.
At least one of these families would need to be fully separated from the rest by a separator of size at most
4kq-1
, which is impossible.

Notice that
\mathcal{L}
is fully contained in the inner graph of
C_{s-s^{\prime}}
.

Next, we need to turn
\mathcal{L}
into a linkage that is orthogonal to the innermost
q
cycles of each area-nest as well as the cycles
C_{s-s^{\prime}-(q-1)},\dots,C_{s-s^{\prime}}
.
For each
i\in[k]
, we apply
lemma

10.2
to
\mathcal{R}_{i}
to obtain a radial linkage
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime}
orthogonal to the cycles
C^{i}_{1},\dots,C^{i}_{5q+1}
and which is end-identical with
\mathcal{R}_{i}
.
Let
X_{i}^{\star}
be the set of first vertices encountered on the cycle
C^{i}_{q+1}
when following along the paths of
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime}
starting from
C^{i}_{1}
and let
X^{\star}=\bigcup_{i\in[k]}X_{i}^{\star}
.
Then, we follow along
\mathcal{L}
, starting from its endpoints on
C_{s-s^{\prime}}
until we meet the cycle
C_{s-s^{\prime}-q-(4kq-1)}
.
Let
\mathcal{F}
be the resulting linkage between
C_{s-s^{\prime}}
and
C_{s-s^{\prime}-q-(4kq-1)}
.
Another application of
lemma

10.2
yields a linkage
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
of the same size which is orthogonal to
\{C_{s-s^{\prime}-q-(4kq-1)},\dots,C_{s-s^{\prime}}\}
and end-identical with
\mathcal{F}
.
Finally, let
Y^{\star}
be the set of all vertices on
C_{s-s^{\prime}-q}
we encounter when traversing along the paths of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
starting from
C_{s-s^{\prime}}
.

Now consider the graph
H^{\star}
obtained by taking the intersection of the inner graph of
C_{s-s^{\prime}-q}
and the outer graphs of the cycles
C^{i}_{q+1}
for all
i\in[k]
– taken in the vortex-free rendition of
(H,\Omega)
that exists according to
Definition

12.1
– by adding the cycles
C_{s-s^{\prime}-q}
and
C^{i}_{q+1}
back in for all
i\in[k]
.
Observe that
H^{\star}
still contains the cycles
C_{s-s^{\prime}-q},\dots,C_{s-s^{\prime}-q-(4kq-1)}
as well as, for each
i\in[k]
, the cycles
C^{i}_{q+1},\dots,C^{i}_{5q+1}
.
It follows, than an application of
proposition

2.1
yields an
X^{\star}
-
Y^{\star}
-linkage
\mathcal{F}^{\star}
of size
4qk
in
H^{\star}
.
We may now combine
\mathcal{F}^{\star}
with the orthogonal linkages
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime}
,
i\in[k]
, and the orthogonal linkage
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
to obtain a linkage
\mathcal{L}^{\star}
whose intersection with any of the cycles
C^{i}_{1},\dots,C^{i}_{q}
for all
i\in[k]
, and the cycles
C_{s-s^{\prime}-(q-1)},\dots,C_{s-s^{\prime}}
is a single, non-empty path.
Moreover,
\mathcal{L}^{\star}
has
4q
endpoints on
C^{i}_{1}
for each
i\in[k]
.

It is now easy to see that
\{C_{s-s^{\prime}},\dots,C_{s-s^{\prime}-(q-1)}\}
together with
\mathcal{L}^{\star}
and the cycles
\{C^{i}_{1},\dots,C^{i}_{q}\}
for each
i\in[k]
form our desired extended
q
-surface-wall.
∎

Combining
lemma

13.2
with the construction of the graph
H^{\prime}_{w,2t}
in extended surface-walls with vortices we have used in the proof of
lemma

5.2
yields a
(w\times 2t)
mesh
M
such that each of our “special” areas is highly connected to its private face in the middle row of
M
.
This allows us to call upon
lemma

4.3
to obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 13.3
.

Let
t,s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell,k
be non-negative integers with
k=2t^{3}-4
,
1\leq t\leq\ell
,
s\geq(4k+5)(2t+24)+s^{\prime}\ell
, and
p\geq 16k(2t+24)
, and let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with an
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P})
.
Moreover, let
\mathcal{A}=\{(G_{i},\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[k]\}
be a selection of
k
pairwise distinct areas of
\mathfrak{M}
.

If there exists a collection
\mathcal{Q}
of
t^{3}-2
pairwise disjoint paths
Q_{j}
in
G
such that
\mathcal{Q}
is a family of
\{V(G_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[k]\}
-paths and each
Q_{j}
is internally disjoint from
H
, then there exists a
K_{t}
-minor model in
G
that is controlled by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct cycles from
\mathcal{C}
.

Moreover, there exists an algorithm that finds this minor model in time
\mathbf{O}(t^{4}|E(G)|)
.

We are now ready for the proof of
theorem

13.1
.

Proof of
Theorem

13.1
.

For each
i\in[\ell+1]
, let
\mathcal{C}_{i}=\{C_{1}^{i},\dots,C^{i}_{s-s^{\prime}\ell-1}\}
be the district nest of
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
.
We set
\mathcal{X}\coloneqq\{C_{1}^{i},C_{2}^{i}~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell+1]\}
,
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\coloneqq\{C_{1}^{i}~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell+1]\}
, and
\mathcal{G}\coloneqq(C_{1}^{1},C_{2}^{1},C_{1}^{2},\ldots,C_{2}^{\ell+1})
.
Notice that each member of
\mathcal{X}
is a cycle and thus the maximum degree of any member of
\mathcal{X}
is
2
.

Suppose
P
is a
\mathcal{X}
-path in
G^{\prime}
.
Then, if there exists some
i\in[\ell+1]
such that
P
has an endpoint in
V(C_{2}^{i})
, the other endpoint of
P
must lie in
V(C_{1}^{i})
, since
C_{1}^{i}
separates
C_{2}^{i}
from all other members of
\mathcal{X}
, since each of our areas has a cylindrical rendition with
\mathcal{C}_{i}
being its nest of grounded cycles.
Thus any
\mathcal{G}
-jump has to be an
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
-path.

We now ask
lemma

6.6
for a subsequence
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{G}
such that there exist
t^{3}-2
pairwise vertex-disjoint
\mathcal{G^{\prime}}
-jumps in
G^{\prime}
with endpoints in
2t^{3}-4
pairwise
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
-independent graphs from
\mathcal{X}
.

If these
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
-jumps exist, let
\mathcal{L}
be the collection of these paths.
According to our observation above,
\mathcal{L}
is also a collection of
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
-paths.
Let
J\subseteq[\ell+1]
be the selection of the
2t^{3}-4
districts that have endpoints of paths from
\mathcal{L}
.
By
corollary

13.3
this means we can find, in time
\mathbf{O}(t^{4}|E(G)|)
, a
K_{t}
-minor model controlled by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct members of
\mathcal{C}
as desired.

Hence, we may assume that such a family
\mathcal{L}
does not exist.
Instead,
lemma

6.6
returns a set
S\subseteq V(G^{\prime})
with
|S|\leq 8t^{3}-16
and a set
\mathcal{Y}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
with
|\mathcal{Y}|\leq 64t^{3}-128
such that every
\mathcal{G}
-jump in
G^{\prime}-S
must have both endpoints in
\bigcup\mathcal{Y}
.

For each
i\in[\ell+1]
, the predecessor of
C_{1}^{i}
in
\mathcal{G}
, if it exists, is
C_{2}^{i-1}
and the successor of
C_{1}^{i}
in
\mathcal{G}
is
C_{2}^{i}
.
Thus any
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
-path in
G^{\prime}-S
is a
\mathcal{G}
-jump and thus must have both endpoints in
\bigcup\mathcal{Y}
.
Furthermore, for every
i\in[\ell+1]
the cycle
C^{i}_{1}
separates
V(\Omega_{i})
from the district vortex of
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
.
Let
I^{\prime}\coloneqq\{i\in[\ell+1]~\!\colon\!~C^{i}_{1}\in\mathcal{Y}\}
.
It follows that in
G^{\prime}-S
every
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell+1]\}
-path must be a
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in I^{\prime}\}
-path.

This implies that no component of
G^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]\setminus I^{\prime}}G_{i}-S
contains vertices of
V(\Omega_{i})
and
V(\Omega_{j})
for any pair of distinct
i,j\in[\ell+1]\setminus I^{\prime}
.
Let
J\subseteq[\ell+1]\setminus I^{\prime}
be the collection of all integers
j\in[\ell+1]\setminus I^{\prime}
such that
(G_{i}-S,\Omega_{i})
has a cross.
If
|J|\geq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)
it follows, via an application of
lemma

13.2
to
\mathfrak{M}
and the selection
J
, followed by an application of
lemma

5.2
, that
G
contains a
K_{t}
-minor model which is controlled by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct cycles from
\mathcal{C}
.
Note that the order of the extended surface wall we require is in
\mathbf{O}(t^{4})
, which further justifies our claimed runtime.
Thus, we may assume
|J|<\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)
.
By setting
I\coloneqq I^{\prime}\cup J
we are done.
∎

14
The society classification theorem

In this section we prove the society classification theorem, which is the key tool to proving the local structure theorem that will itself appear in
Section

15
.
Before we state it, let us briefly give an imprecise intuition behind this theorem.
It says that given a cylindrical rendition of a society with a sufficiently large nest with a radial linkage, we can guarantee one of four outcomes:

i)

We find a
K_{t}
-minor that attaches conveniently to a mesh we find in our nest,

ii)

we find a large crosscap transaction (under a small set of apices),

iii)

we find a large handle transaction (under a small set of apices), or

iv)

we can completely decompose the entire vortex into a few vortices of bounded depth (using a larger, but still relatively small set of apices) and these vortices are themselves surrounded by nests that attach neatly to an extended surface-wall.

The name of the theorem derives from the idea that it classifies societies into one of four options, each of which is in some way beneficial to us.
We will give estimates on the order of the functions involved in this theorem in its proof in
Section

14.4
.

Theorem 14.1

(Society classification theorem)
.

There exist polynomial functions
\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}},\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}\colon\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}},\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
, and
\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}
,
\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{3}\to\mathbb{N}
, such that the following holds.

Let
t,s,p,k
be positive integers with
s\geq\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k)
.
Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with a cylindrical rendition
\rho
in a disk
\Delta
, a cozy nest
\mathcal{C}=\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{s}\}
of order
s
in
\rho
around the vortex
c_{0}
.
Further, let
(G^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})
be the
C_{s-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k)}
-society in
\rho
.

Then
G^{\prime}
contains a set
A\subseteq V(G^{\prime})
such that one of the following exists:

i)

A
K_{t}
-minor model in
G
controlled by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct cycles from
\mathcal{C}
.

ii)

A flat crosscap transaction
\mathcal{P}
of order
p
in
(G^{\prime}-A,\Omega^{\prime})
, with
|A|\leq\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)
, and a nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
\rho
of order
s-(\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)+\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k))
around
c_{0}
to which
\mathcal{P}
is orthogonal.

iii)

A flat handle transaction
\mathcal{P}
of order
p
in
(G^{\prime}-A,\Omega^{\prime})
, with
|A|\leq\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)
, and a nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
\rho
of order
s-(\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)+\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k))
around
c_{0}
to which
\mathcal{P}
is orthogonal.

iv)

A rendition
\rho^{\prime}
of
(G-A,\Omega)
in
\Delta
with breadth
b\in[\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)-1]
and depth at most
\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k,p)
,
|A|\leq\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k,p)
, and an extended
k
-surface-wall
D
with signature
(0,0,b)
, such that
D
is grounded in
\rho^{\prime}
, the base cycles of
D
are the cycles
C_{s-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k)-1-k},\dots,C_{s-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k)-1}
, and there exists a bijection between the vortices
v
of
\rho^{\prime}
and the vortex segments
S_{v}
of
D
, where
v
is the unique vortex contained in the disk
\Delta_{S_{v}}
defined by the trace of the inner cycle of the nest of
S_{v}
where
\Delta_{S_{v}}
is chosen to avoid the trace of the simple cycle of
D
.

In particular, the set
A
, the
K_{t}
-minor model, the transaction
\mathcal{P}
, the rendition
\rho^{\prime}
, and the extended surface-wall
D
can each be found in time
\mathbf{poly}(t+s+p+k)|E(G)||V(G)|^{2}
.

Before we delve into the quite extensive procedure to prove this theorem, we give a rough guide to our approach.
In the broadest of terms, we will simply try to build a transaction mesh in
(G,\Omega)
and along the way, if we fail to keep building the transaction mesh, we will find the four outcomes stated in the theorem.

The actual proof is then split into three parts:

(i)
We have to show that we can do something with a transaction mesh that we know cannot be extended anymore, meaning that we can no longer find transaction large enough to increase the transaction mesh for one more iteration.
This will actually be the exclusive source for the last two options of the statement.
(See
Section

14.1
for this part.)

(ii)
If we do end up finding a big transaction that looks promising, we have to show that we can actually build a new transaction mesh using it.
This in particular means we have to delve into the internals of
Definition

12.1
and also
Definition

12.14
, as the
\mathfrak{M}
-tree will be crucial to the third part of the proof.
(See
Section

14.2
for this part.)

(iii)
Should we never run into the first part and instead keep finding infrastructure to increase the order of our transaction mesh, we also run into trouble.
Since we lose a little bit of the transactions and in particular, the nest of the society we started with, at some point this process will destroy all that we have built.
Thus, in this last part, we show that there is actually an explicit bound on the order of a transaction mesh we can build with our methods without encountering a
K_{t}
-minor model.
(See
Section

14.3
for this part).

The proof that puts all of the intermediate results we find in these subsections together and ultimately yields
Theorem

14.1
is then found in
Section

14.4
.

14.1
What to do when every district has bounded depth

Though the fourth option of
Theorem

14.1
may seem to be the most involved at first glance, we will actually discuss it first as it is the option that has the lowest requirement for analysis of the internals of
Definition

12.1
and
Definition

12.14
.
Mainly, we are interested in how to use linkages that jump between districts of our transaction mesh to find handle or crosscap transactions and if we cannot find such a linkage, we separate all districts from each other and employ the tools from
Section

13
to verify the details of the fourth item of
Theorem

14.1
.

This explanation is of course an oversimplification.
As we will see later, one of the principle technical hurdles in this subsection will be that we need to simultaneously orthogonalise two flat transactions within the same society.
This is used in the one spot of our proof in which handle transactions can appear.
Surprisingly, the machinery we have developed earlier is not quite enough to solve this problem.
Instead we will have to argue ad hoc that this is possible, though the methods we use then again yield quite modest bounds on the amount of paths from the transactions and cycles from the nest this costs us.

We will start by slightly generalising
Lemma

10.6
to linkages that connect two societies, which requires us to first define such linkages.

Definition 14.2

(Connecting linkages)
.

Let
G
be a graph and let
(G_{1},\Omega_{1}),(G_{2},\Omega_{2})
be two societies with
G_{1},G_{2}\subseteq G
and
V(\Omega_{1})\cap V(\Omega_{2})=\emptyset
, such that for both
i\in[2]
there exists a cylindrical rendition of
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
in a disk with a nest
\mathcal{C}_{i}
around a vortex
c_{i}
and we have
G_{i}\subseteq\sigma_{\rho_{3-i}}(c_{3-i})-\widetilde{c}_{3-i}
.

A
V(\Omega_{1})
-
V(\Omega_{2})
-linkage
\mathcal{P}
in
G
is called a
connecting linkage (between
(G_{1},\Omega_{1})
and
(G_{2},\Omega_{2})
)
.
We call
\mathcal{P}

orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
if the intersection of each path of
\mathcal{P}
with each cycle of
\mathcal{C}_{i}
, for both
i\in[2]
, consists of a single, non-empty path.
The
end segments of
\mathcal{P}
are two minimal segments of
\Omega_{1}
and
\Omega_{2}
that contain the endpoints of
\mathcal{P}
, which are unique as long as
\mathcal{P}
has order at least 3.

The proof for the next lemma again follows the proof of
Lemma

10.6
closely.
Thus we only sketch the differences, as in the proofs of similar lemmas throughout this article.

Lemma 14.3
.

Let
s,p,q
be positive integers and let
k
be a non-negative integer.
Let
\{((G_{i},\Omega_{i}),\rho_{i},c_{i},\Delta_{i},\mathcal{C}_{i})\}_{i\in[0,k]}
be a full
(s,k)
-society configuration, such that for each
i\in[0,k]
the tuple
(\mathcal{C}_{i},\mathfrak{P}_{i})
is an
(s,p,\ell_{i})
-padding for
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
with
\ell_{i}\leq\ell
and the inner nest
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime}=\{C_{1}^{i},\ldots,C_{s}^{i}\}
.
Further, let
\mathcal{P}
be a connecting linkage in
G
of order
p(q-1)+1
between
(G_{0},\Omega_{0})
and
(G_{1},\Omega_{1})
with the end segments
X_{0},X_{1}
such that
X_{i}\subseteq V(\Omega_{i})
for both
i\in\{0,1\}
.

Then there exists a connecting linkage
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
in
G
of order
q
between
(G_{0},\Omega_{0})
and
(G_{1},\Omega_{1})
such that

i)

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is orthogonal to
\{C_{2}^{i},\dots,C_{s}^{i}\}
for both
i\in\{0,1\}
,

ii)

\bigcup\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is disjoint from the union of
C_{2}^{i}
and the outer graph of
C_{2}^{i}
in
\rho_{i}
for all
i\in[2,k]
, and

iii)

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
connects vertices of
X_{0}\cap V(\mathcal{P})
to vertices of
X_{1}\cap V(\mathcal{P})
.

Moreover, there exists an algorithm that finds
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
in time
\mathbf{O}((p+k)|E(G)|)
.

Proof sketch.

For all
i\in[0,k]
, let
H_{i}
be the graph derived from outer graph of
C_{1}^{i}
in
\rho_{i}
by deleting
V(C_{1}^{i})
.
We let
G^{\prime}
be
G-V(\bigcup_{i=0}^{k}H_{i})
.
For both
i\in\{0,1\}
and each
P\in\mathcal{P}
let
P_{i}
be the unique
X_{i}
-
C_{1}^{i}
-path in
P
.
We set
\mathcal{L}_{i}=\{P_{i}~\!\colon\!~P\in\mathcal{P}\}
, for both
i\in\{0,1\}
, and let
X_{i}^{\prime}
be the set of all endpoints of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
in
V(C_{1}^{i})
.
By applying
Lemma

10.2
to both
\mathcal{L}_{0}
and
\mathcal{L}_{1}
, we find two new radial linkages
\mathcal{L}_{0}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}
, such that
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\prime}
is an
X_{i}
-
X_{i}^{\prime}
-linkage and
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\prime}
is orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}_{i}
for both
i\in\{0,1\}
.

If we now apply
Menger’s Theorem
in
G^{\prime}
to find an
X_{0}^{\prime}
-
X_{1}^{\prime}
-linkage
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
of order
q
, then we are done if we actually find
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, via arguments analogous to those presented in the proof of
Lemma

10.6
.
Thus we must instead find an
X_{0}^{\prime}
-
X_{1}^{\prime}
-separation
(A,B)
of order at most
q-1
in
G^{\prime}
.
We let
S\coloneq A\cap B
and for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
, we let
\widetilde{P}
be the unique
X_{0}^{\prime}
-
X_{1}^{\prime}
-subpath of
P
, with
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}
being the collection of all paths
\widetilde{P}
.
By definition
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}
is an
X_{0}^{\prime}
-
X_{1}^{\prime}
-linkage of order
(p-1)(q-1)+1
in
G
.

We may now define gangplanks and gangboards as in the proof of
Lemma

11.2
, in reference to
c_{i}
and
\rho_{i}
if the path
\widetilde{P}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}
intersects
C_{1}^{i}
for
i\in[0,k]
.
If there existed a path
\widetilde{P}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}
such that the path and all of its gangplanks avoided
S
, we would reach a contradiction to
S
being a separator.
Thus, just as in the proof of
Lemma

10.6
and
Lemma

12.8
, we note that there must exist some vertex
u\in S
which is contained in the gangplanks of at least
p
distinct paths from
\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}
.
This circumstances now leads us towards a contradiction in exactly the same fashion as it did in our proof of
Lemma

12.8
.
∎

The principal agenda of this subsection is to establish a small set of tools to handle certain routing problems of radial linkages within a padded transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}
.
The purpose of these tools is to allow for the projection of a large crosscap transaction within a district of
\mathfrak{M}
back to the original society of the transaction mesh.
Moreover, sometimes we will find large linkages between two distinct districts of
\mathfrak{M}
and we will also have to be able to route such linkages back onto the original society while, at the same time, ensuring the existence of a big transaction within the infrastructure of
\mathfrak{M}
itself that separates the two districts while being fully disjoint from the newly routed linkages.
This second part is crucial for the construction of a handle transaction.
The main difficulty here also reveals the sole reason for our introduction of paddings:
In later steps of our proofs we will need these linkages to be much larger than the original nest to avoid an exponential blow-up of our bounds.
To ensure this, it is not enough to rely on the infrastructure provided by the regular district nests, instead we require the full power of the padding to provide enough connectivity for the routing of such large linkages.

Let
s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell,k
be non-negative integers with
\max(a,s^{\prime})\leq s
and
2s+2-(s^{\prime}\ell)\leq p
.
Let
\mathfrak{M}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P}=\{\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell}\})
be a padded
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh in the society
(G,\Omega)
.
Finally, let
k\in[\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell]
.
The
k
-splitstream
of
\mathfrak{M}
is the pair
(\mathfrak{S}_{k},\mathfrak{T}_{k})
where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{k}

\displaystyle\coloneq\big\{\mathcal{S}_{i}=\{P^{i}_{j}\colon j\in[k]\cup[p-k+1,p]\}\colon i\in[\ell]\big\}\text{, and}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{k}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\big\{\mathcal{S}_{i}=\{P^{i}_{j}\colon j\in[k+1,\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell]\cup[\lceil\nicefrac{{(p+1)}}{{2}}\rceil-(s^{\prime}\ell+k),p-k]\}\colon i\in[\ell]\big\}.

We call
\mathcal{S}_{k}
the
surface stream
and
\mathcal{T}_{k}
the
deep stream
of the
k
-splitstream of
\mathfrak{M}
.

Notice, that for
\mathfrak{X}\in\{\mathfrak{S},\mathfrak{T}\}
, the graph
X=\bigcup\mathfrak{X}_{k}
is not necessarily connected.
However, from any vertex
v\in V(X)
there is a path in
X
from
v
to
V(\Omega)
.

We begin by showing that a large radial linkage in a district of
\mathcal{M}
can – essentially – be projected onto the original society by only using an appropriately sized surface stream of
\mathfrak{M}
.

Lemma 14.4
.

Let
t,s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell,k
be non-negative integers with
\max(a,s^{\prime})\ell\leq s
,
2s+2-(s^{\prime}\ell)\leq p
, and
k\in[\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell]
.
Let
\mathfrak{M}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P}=\{\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell}\})
be a padded
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh in the society
(G,\Omega)
with districts
\{(G_{i},\Omega_{i})\}_{i\in[\ell+1]}
and cylindrical rendition
\rho
with nest
\mathcal{C}
.
Moreover, let
i\in[\ell+1]
and
(\mathcal{C}_{i},\mathfrak{P}_{i})
be the padding of
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
and
\mathcal{R}
be a radial linkage of order at least
k
in
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
.
Finally, let
X
be the set of endpoints of
\mathcal{R}
on the innermost cycle of
(\mathcal{C}_{i},\mathfrak{P}_{i})
and let
(G^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})
be the
C_{s-s^{\prime}\ell}
-society in
\rho
.

Then there exists an
X
-
V(\Omega^{\prime})
-linkage
\mathcal{L}
of order
k
in
\bigcup\mathcal{C}\cup\bigcup\mathfrak{S}_{k}\cup\bigcup\mathcal{R}
where
\mathfrak{S}_{k}
is the surface stream of the
k
-splitstream of
\mathfrak{M}
.
Moreover, the linkage
\mathcal{L}
can be found in time
\mathbf{O}(k|E(G)|)
.

Proof.

Let
H^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigcup\mathcal{C}\cup\bigcup\mathfrak{S}_{k}\cup\bigcup\mathcal{R}
.
Suppose there does not exist an
X
-
V(G)
-linkage
\mathcal{L}
of order
k
in
G^{\prime}
.
Then, by
proposition

2.1
, there exists a set
Y
of size at most
k-1
such that there is no path from
X
to
V(\Omega)
in
H^{\prime}-Y
.
It follows that there exists a path
R\in\mathcal{R}
that is disjoint from
Y
.
By choice of
Y
we know that
R
cannot have an endpoint on
V(\Omega^{\prime})
.
It follows that there exists
\iota\in[\ell]
such that the endpoint of
R
that is not in
X
belongs to a path of
\mathcal{P}_{\iota}
.
Hence,
\mathcal{R}
intersects
k
paths of the sublinkage
\mathcal{S}_{\iota}\in\mathfrak{S}_{k}
of
\mathcal{P}_{\iota}
.

Now notice that there exists a sequence
\sigma=\langle\mathcal{Z}_{i_{j}}\rangle_{j\in[\ell^{\prime}]}
,
\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell
and
i_{j}\in[\ell]
for all
j\in[\ell^{\prime}]
such that

i)

for each
i\in[\ell^{\prime}]
,
\mathcal{Z}_{i_{j}}\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{i_{j}}
is a linkage of order
k
,

ii)

i_{1}=\iota
,

iii)

each path in
\mathcal{Z}_{i_{\ell^{\prime}}}
contains a vertex of
V(\Omega^{\prime})
, and

iv)

for all
j\in[\ell^{\prime}-1]
, every path of
\mathcal{Z}_{i_{j}}
intersects every path of
\mathcal{Z}_{i_{j+1}}
.

The existence of
\sigma
follows directly from the definition of transaction meshes and
k
-splitstreams.
Now, however, as
|Y|\leq k-1
, each
\mathcal{Z}_{i_{j}}
contains a path
Z_{i_{j}}
disjoint from
Y
.
It follows that
R\cup\bigcup_{j\in[\ell^{\prime}]}Z_{i_{j}}
is connected, disjoint from
Y
and contains a vertex of both
X
and
V(\Omega^{\prime})
.
Hence, there exists an
X
-
V(\Omega^{\prime})
-path in
H^{\prime}-Y
which is absurd.

Therefore, we must be able to find our desired linkage
\mathcal{L}
by applying
proposition

2.1
.
∎

Next, we will show that, given a transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}
, a
k
-splitstream
(\mathfrak{S},\mathfrak{T})
of
\mathfrak{M}
, and two “special” districts
A
and
B
of
\mathfrak{M}
we can always find a large transaction on
(G,\Omega)
which separates
A
and
B
in
\mathfrak{M}
and is entirely contained in the deep stream of
(\mathfrak{S},\mathfrak{T})
.
This transaction will be the base for the construction of handle transactions later on.

Lemma 14.5
.

Let
t,s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell,k
be non-negative integers with
\max(a,s^{\prime})\ell\leq s
,
2s+2-(s^{\prime}\ell)\leq p
, and
k\in[\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell]
.
Let
\mathfrak{M}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P}=\{\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell}\})
be a padded
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh in the society
(G,\Omega)
with districts
\{(G_{i},\Omega_{i})\}_{i\in[\ell+1]}
and cylindrical rendition
\rho
with nest
\mathcal{C}
.

Let
i_{1},i_{2}\in[\ell+1]
be distinct and let
(H^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})
the
C_{s-s^{\prime}\ell}
-society in
\rho
with respect to
H
, i.e.
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
.
Then there exists a transaction
\mathcal{Q}
of order
h\coloneqq\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell-k
in
(H^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})
that separates the areas
H_{i_{1}}
and
H_{i_{2}}
in
H
where
(H_{i_{1}},\Omega_{i_{1}})
and
(H_{i_{2}},\Omega_{i_{2}})
are distinct areas of
\mathfrak{M}
such that
\mathcal{Q}
is completely contained in the deep stream of the
k
-splitstream of
\mathfrak{M}
.
Moreover, the transaction
\mathcal{Q}
can be found in time
\mathbf{O}(p|E(G)|)
.

Proof.

We will iteratively construct a sequence of constrictions
(B_{i},\Lambda_{i})
of the society
(H^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})
by considering the linkages
\mathcal{P}_{i}
one at a time.
Each time, we add
\mathcal{P}_{i+1}
to
(B_{i},\Lambda_{i},\mathfrak{L}_{i})
we ask if
\mathcal{P}_{i+1}
separates
H_{i+1}
and
H_{i+2}
from one another in
B_{i}
.
If this is the case we make use of the linkages constricting
(B_{i},\Lambda_{i})
to extend
\mathcal{P}_{i+1}
back to
V(\Lambda_{i})
.
Otherwise, we know that one of the two “sides” of
\mathcal{P_{i+1}}
in
B_{i}
is fully disjoint from both of our special areas.
In this case, we can further restrict the society.
Since
H_{i_{1}}
and
H_{i_{2}}
define distinct areas in
\mathfrak{M}
, this process must eventually find a large transaction
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
separating both.
At this point it suffices to restrict the attention to the linkages found in the deep stream which correspond to the linkages used in the construction of
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
in order to obtain the desired transaction
\mathcal{Q}
.

Notice that, by definition of transaction meshes, there exists a vortex-free rendition
\rho^{\prime}
of
(H^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})
in the disk.
We set
(B_{0},\Lambda_{0},\emptyset)\coloneqq(H^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime},\emptyset)
.

Now, suppose for some
j\in[0,\ell-1]
we have already constructed the
(\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell,z_{j})
-constriction
(B_{j},\Lambda_{j},\mathfrak{L}_{j})
of
(B_{0},\Lambda_{0})
with
z_{j}\in[0,j]
satisfying the following properties:

i)

we may index the set
\mathfrak{L}_{j}=\{\mathcal{L}_{i}\colon i\in I_{j}\subseteq[j]\}
such that every path of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
is a subpath of some path in
\mathcal{P}_{i}
where
|I_{j}|=z_{j}
,

ii)

for each
i\in[j]\setminus I_{j}
,
\mathcal{P}_{i}
is disjoint from
B_{j}
,

iii)

for all
i\in[j+1,\ell]
,
\mathcal{P}_{i}
is either fully contained in
B_{j}
or disjoint from
B_{j}
, and

iv)

H_{i_{x}}\subseteq B_{j}
for both
x\in[2]
.

Since both
H_{i_{1}}
and
H_{i_{2}}
are contained in
B_{j}
there must be some
\iota\in[j+1,\ell]
such that
\mathcal{P}_{\iota}
is contained in
B_{j}
.
Let us select
\iota
to be the smallest such index, then
\mathcal{P}_{\iota}
is a transaction on
(B_{j},\Lambda_{j})
.
We consider two cases.

Case 1:

\mathcal{P}_{\iota}
separates
H_{i_{1}}
and
H_{i_{2}}
in
H
.

In this case let
\mathfrak{T}_{k}
be the deep stream of the
k
-splitstream of
\mathfrak{M}
.
Notice that
\mathcal{T}_{\iota}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{\iota}
, i.e. the subtransaction of
\mathcal{P}_{\iota}
that makes up the deep stream, still separates
H_{i_{1}}
and
H_{i_{2}}
in
B_{j}
.
Moreover, it is now straight forward to see that there exists a transaction
\mathcal{Q}\subseteq\bigcup\mathfrak{T}_{k}
of order
\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell-k=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}|\mathcal{T}_{\iota}|
on
(B_{0},\Lambda_{0})
that contains half of the paths of
\mathcal{S}_{\iota}
.

We make two observations on
\mathcal{Q}
.

First,
\mathcal{Q}
can be found inductively by first finding a corresponding transaction
\mathcal{Q}_{j}
in
(B_{j},\Lambda_{j})
and then extending
\mathcal{Q}_{j}
towards
V(\Lambda_{0})
by retracing the sequence of constrictions we constructed so far.

Second,
\mathcal{Q}
separates
H_{i_{1}}
and
H_{i_{2}}
in
B_{0}=H^{\prime}
.

Hence, in this case we have found the desired transaction and may conclude.

Case 2:

\mathcal{P}_{\iota}
does not separate
H_{i_{1}}
and
H_{i_{2}}
in
H
.

In this case there exists
y\in\{1,p\}
such that every
V(H_{i_{1}})
-
V(P^{\iota}_{p+1-y})
-path in
H
intersects
P^{\iota}_{y}
.
Without loss of generality we may assume
y=1
.
We now define
\mathcal{L}_{\iota}^{\prime}\coloneqq\{P^{\iota}_{1},\dots,P^{\iota}_{\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell}\}
.
Consider the path
R=P^{\iota}_{\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell+1}
.
Still,
R
separates both
H_{i_{x}}
,
x\in[2]
, from
P^{\iota}_{p}
.
Indeed, if we consider the restriction
\rho_{j}
of
\rho^{\prime}
to the society
(B_{j},\Lambda_{j})
, then
\rho_{j}
is a vortex-free rendition of
(B_{j},\Lambda_{j})
in a disk
\Delta_{j}
and
R
is grounded in
\rho_{j}
.
Let
\gamma
be the trace of
R
.
Then
\gamma
separates
\Delta_{j}
into a disk
\Delta_{\iota}
and a disk
\Delta^{\prime}
where
\Delta_{\iota}
contains both
H_{i_{1}}
and
H_{i_{2}}
.
Let
B_{\iota}
be the subgraph of
B_{j}
drawn on
\Delta_{\iota}
and let
\Lambda_{\iota}
be obtained from the segment of
\Lambda_{j}
whose vertices are drawn on
\Delta_{\iota}
by extending the order by the natural order of the ground vertices of
R
.
Finally, for each
i\in I_{j}
let
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j}
be the restriction of
\mathcal{L}_{j}
to
\Delta_{\iota}
and let
I_{\iota}\coloneqq\{\iota\}\cup\{i\in I_{j}\colon\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i}\neq\emptyset\}
.
It follows that
(B_{\iota},\Lambda_{\iota},\{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i}\colon i\in I_{\iota}\})
is the desired constriction of
(B_{0},\Lambda_{0})
.
∎

Having now found a large transaction that separates two districts
A
and
B
in the graph
H
from the transaction mesh, we want to simultaneously route both
A
and
B
back onto the society with linkages that are disjoint from the transaction that separates them.

Lemma 14.6
.

Let
t,s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell,k
be non-negative integers with
\max(a,s^{\prime})\ell\leq s
,
2s+2-s^{\prime}\ell\leq p
, and
k\in[\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell]
such that
\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell\geq 3k+1
.
Let
\mathfrak{M}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P}=\{\mathcal{P}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell}\})
be a padded
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh in the society
(G,\Omega)
with districts
\{(G_{i},\Omega_{i})\}_{i\in[\ell+1]}
and a cylindrical rendition
\rho
with nest
\mathcal{C}
.

Let
i_{1},i_{2}\in[\ell+1]
be distinct and for each
j\in[2]
, let
\mathcal{R}_{j}
be a radial linkage in
(G_{i_{j}},\Omega_{i_{j}})
of order
k
.
Let
(H_{i_{j}},\Omega_{i_{j}})
be the area corresponding to the district
(G_{i_{j}},\Omega_{i_{j}})
for both
j\in[2]
.

Finally, let
(H^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})
the
C_{s-s^{\prime}\ell}
-society in
\rho
with respect to
H
and let
G^{\prime}
be the inner graph of
C_{s-s^{\prime}\ell}
.
Then there exist the following three objects:

i)

a transaction
\mathcal{Q}
of order
\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell-3k
on
(H^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})
that separates
H_{i_{1}}
and
H_{i_{2}}
in
H
, and

ii)

two linkages
\mathcal{L}_{i}
in
H
,
i\in[2]
, each of order
k
such that for each
i\in[2]
,
\mathcal{L}_{i}
is a linkage between
V(\Omega^{\prime})
and the endpoints of
\mathcal{R}_{i}
on the inner cycle of
(\mathcal{C}_{i_{j}},\mathfrak{P}_{i_{j}})
.

Moreover,
V(\mathcal{Q})
,
V(\mathcal{L}_{1})
, and
V(\mathcal{L}_{2})
are pairwise disjoint and the linkages
\mathcal{Q}
,
\mathcal{L}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
can be found in time
\mathbf{O}(p|E(G)|)
.

Proof.

We begin with two applications of
lemma

14.4
to the radial linkages
\mathcal{R}_{1}
and
\mathcal{R}_{2}
.
As a result, we obtain two linkages
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\prime}
such that for each
j\in[2]
,
|\mathcal{R}_{j}|=|\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}|
,
the endpoints of
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}
not on
C{s-s^{\prime}\ell}
are precisely the endpoints of
\mathcal{R}_{j}
on the inner cycle of
(G_{i_{j}},\Omega_{i_{j}})
, and
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}
is completely contained within the surface stream of the
k
-splitstream of
\mathfrak{M}
.

So far, we have two good candidates for the final linkages we are looking for.
However, we are missing the transaction on
(G^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})
that separates our two special districts, and at this stage we have no guarantee that
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\prime}
are disjoint.

Next, we apply
lemma

14.5
to
\mathfrak{M}
and the two areas
(H_{i_{1}},\Omega_{i_{1}})
and
(H_{i_{2}},\Omega_{i_{2}})
using the same
k
to split the stream.
The result is a transaction
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}=\{Q_{1},\dots,Q_{\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell-k}\}
, indexed naturally such that
Q_{1}
separates all other paths in
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
from
H_{i_{1}}
in
H
, which is fully contained in the deep stream of the
k
-splitstream of
\mathfrak{M}
and which separates
(H_{i_{1}},\Omega_{i_{1}})
and
(H_{i_{2}},\Omega_{i_{2}})
in
H
.

What is left is to adjust
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\prime}
.
We define three subtransactions of
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
as follows.

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{Q_{1},\dots,Q_{k}\}

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{Q_{k+1},\dots,Q_{\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell-2k}\}\text{, and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{Q_{\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell-2k+1},\dots,Q_{\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell-k}\}

Then
\mathcal{Q}
is the desired transaction of order
\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-s^{\prime}\ell-3k\geq 1
as required by the statement of our lemma.

For each
j\in[2]
let
H^{\prime}_{j}
be the component of
H-\bigcup\mathcal{Q}
that contains
H_{i_{j}}
.
Now for each
j\in[2]
consider the graph

\displaystyle F_{j}\coloneqq\big(\bigcup\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}\cap H_{j}\big)\cup\bigcup\mathcal{Q}_{j}

and let
X_{j}
be the endpoints of
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}
on the inner cycle of
(G_{i_{j}},\Omega_{i_{,}j})
, while
Y_{j}
is the set of remaining endpoints of
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}
on
V(\Omega^{\prime})\cap V(H_{j})
together with the set of endpoints of
\mathcal{Q}_{j}
(which all belong to
V(\Omega^{\prime})\cap H_{j}
).

We claim that for both
j\in[2]
there is an
X_{j}
-
Y_{j}
-linkage of order
k
in
F_{j}
.
To see this, suppose there is no such linkage.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
j=1
since the arguments are fully symmetric.
Then
proposition

2.1
yields the existence of a set
S_{1}
of order at most
k-1
such that
F_{1}
does not have any
X_{1}
-
Y_{1}
-path.
Since
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}
has order
k
there is a path
L\in\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\prime}
which is disjoint from
S_{1}
.
If
L\subseteq H_{1}
then we would have a contradiction to our choice of
S_{1}
, hence
L
must contain a vertex that does not belong to
H_{1}
.
It follows, that there exists an
X_{1}
-
V(Q_{k+1})
-subpath
L^{\prime}
of
L
.
Since
(H,\Omega)
has a vortex-free rendition in the disk we must have that
L^{\prime}
intersects every path in
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
.
With
|\mathcal{Q}_{1}|=k
it holds, however, that there is some
P\in\mathcal{Q}_{1}
which is disjoint from
S_{1}
.
Since
P
contains a vertex of
V(\Omega^{\prime})\cap V(H_{1})
and
L^{\prime}
intersects
P
, we have now found an
X_{1}
-
Y_{1}
-path in
F_{1}-S_{1}
which is absurd.
Hence, for each
j\in[2]
, an application of
proposition

2.1
yields the desired linkage
\mathcal{L}_{j}
and our proof is complete.
∎

The next statement is quite substantial in size, but it intuitively breaks down to the following:
If all of the districts of the transaction mesh already have bounded depth and we attempt to separate them from each other, we will either find a handle or crosscap transaction by utilising
lemma

14.6
, or we are able to construct the rendition that we seek in the last point of
Theorem

14.1
.
For the latter option most of the technical aspects are handled by
Lemma

13.2
.

This lemma features all items from
Theorem

14.1
and will actually be the only way in which we reach the last two points of the theorem in our proof.
The case which produces a handle will give us a lot of grief, as noted at the beginning of this section.

Lemma 14.7
.

There exist functions
\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}
,
\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}\colon\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
\mathsf{link}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{3}\to\mathbb{N}
, and
\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{4}\to\mathbb{N}
with
\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(x)
,
\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(x)
,
\mathsf{link}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(x,y,z)
.
\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(w,x,y,z)\in\mathbf{poly}(w+x+y+z)
for all integers
x,y,z
, such that the following holds.

Let
t,s,k,a,a^{\prime},p,d,q
be non-negative and
\ell
be positive integers with
p\geq 2s+2-k(\ell+1)+\mathsf{link}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t,s,q)
, and
s\geq\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t)+a+k(\ell+1)+1
.
Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with a cylindrical rendition
\rho
in a disk
\Delta
, a cozy nest
\mathcal{C}=\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{s}\}
of order
s
in
\rho
around the vortex
c_{0}
, and an
(s,k,a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}
, such that the union of the apices for the transactions in
\mathfrak{P}
have order at most
a^{\prime}
.
Further, let
\{(G_{i},\Omega_{i})\}_{i\in[\ell+1]}
be the districts of
\mathfrak{M}
, with cylindrical renditions
\rho_{i}
and nests
\mathcal{C}_{i}=\{C_{1}^{i},\ldots,C_{s-(k\ell+1)}^{i}\}
.
Further, let
(G^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime})
be the
C_{s-k(\ell+1)}
-society in
\rho
.

If for all
i\in[\ell+1]
the
C_{s-k(\ell+1)}^{i}
-society
(G_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
of
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
in
\rho_{i}
has depth at most
d
, then there exists a set
A\subseteq V(G)
and one of the following holds:

i)

a
K_{t}
-minor model in
G
controlled by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct cycles from
\mathcal{C}
(in this case
A=\emptyset
),

ii)

we have
|A|\leq 8t^{3}+a^{\prime}
and there exists a flat crosscap transaction
\mathcal{P}
of order
q
in
(G^{\prime\prime}-A,\Omega^{\prime\prime})
, and a nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
\rho
of order
s-(\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t)+k(\ell+1)+1)
around
c_{0}
to which
\mathcal{P}
is orthogonal,

iii)

we have
|A|\leq 8t^{3}+a^{\prime}
and there exists a flat handle transaction
\mathcal{P}
of order
q
in
(G^{\prime\prime}-A,\Omega^{\prime\prime})
, and a nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
\rho
of order
s-(\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t)+k(\ell+1)+1)
around
c_{0}
to which
\mathcal{P}
is orthogonal, or

iv)

we have
|A|\leq\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t,s,p,q)
and there exists a rendition
\rho^{\prime}
of
(G-A,\Omega)
in
\Delta
with breadth
b\in[\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)-1]
and depth at most
d
, and an extended
k
-surface-wall
D
with signature
(0,0,b)
, such that
D
is grounded in
\rho^{\prime}
, the base cycles of
D
are the cycles
C_{s-k\ell-1-k},\dots,C_{s-k\ell-1}
, and there exists a bijection between the vortices
v
of
\rho^{\prime}
and the vortex segments
S_{v}
of
D
, where
v
is the unique vortex contained in the disk
\Delta_{S_{v}}
defined by the trace of the inner cycle of the nest of
S_{v}
where
\Delta_{S_{v}}
is chosen to avoid the trace of the simple cycle of
D
.

In particular, the set
A
, the
K_{t}
-minor model, the transaction
\mathcal{P}
, the rendition
\rho^{\prime}
, and the extended surface-wall
D
can each be found in time
\mathbf{poly}(t+k+p+q+s)|E(G)|
.

Proof.

We give the following estimate on the functions from the statement of our claims including one function we use for auxiliary purposes.

\displaystyle\mathsf{link}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t,s,q)

\displaystyle\coloneqq 2s(q+1)+16t^{3}+6\big(s\cdot((97t^{3}(q+40))^{2}+2)+8s+1-1\big)+1

\displaystyle\mathsf{aux\text{-}link}(t,s,p,q)

\displaystyle\coloneqq p\cdot\big(s\cdot((97t^{3}(q+40))^{2}+2)+8s\big)+1

\displaystyle\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t,s,p,q)

\displaystyle\coloneqq 8t^{3}+(64t^{3}-128)^{2}\cdot\mathsf{aux\text{-}link}(t,p,q)-16\in\mathbf{O}(t^{12}psq^{2})

\displaystyle\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneqq 16t+8t^{3}+10

\displaystyle\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t)

\displaystyle\coloneq 8t^{3}+10

We start by applying
Theorem

13.1
.
If this yields a
K_{t}
-minor model then it also has the extra properties we seek and thus we are done.
As a consequence we may suppose that we instead find sets
S\subseteq V(G^{\prime})
and
I=I_{1}\cup I_{2}\subseteq[\ell+1]
with
|S|\leq 8t^{3}-16
,
|I_{1}|\leq 64t^{3}-128
, and
|I_{2}|<\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)
such that each
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell+1]\}
-path in
G^{\prime}-S
is a
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in I_{1}\}
-path and each district
(G_{j}-S,\Omega_{j})
with
j\in[\ell+1]\setminus(I_{1}\cup I_{2})
has a vortex-free rendition in a disk.

Let
G^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]}G_{i}^{\prime}
.
For all
i\neq j\in I_{1}
, we apply
Menger’s theorem
to find a
V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
-
V(\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
-linkage of order at least
\mathsf{aux\text{-}link}(t,s,p,q)
in
G^{\prime}
.

Let us first discuss the case where none of these applications of
proposition

2.1
yield the desired linkage.

In this case, we may assume that after at most
|I_{1}|^{2}
iterations, we have found a
V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
-
V(\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
-separator
S_{i,j}
of size a most
\mathsf{aux\text{-}link}(t,s,p,q)
for any two distinct
i,j\in I_{1}
.
Let
S^{\prime}\coloneq S\cup\bigcup_{i\in I_{1}}\bigcup_{j\in I_{1}\setminus\{i\}}S_{i,j}
and note that we have
|S|\leq|S|+\mathsf{aux\text{-}link}(t,s,p,q)|I_{1}|^{2}\leq\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t,s,p,q)
.
Moreover, it follows that in
G^{\prime}-S^{\prime}
there do not exists any
V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
-
V(\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
-paths for
i,j\in[\ell+1]
with
i\neq j
.

We now update our graphs as follows.
For each
i\in[\ell+1]
let
\Omega_{i}^{\prime\prime}
be the restriction of
\Omega_{i}^{\prime}
to
V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})\setminus S^{\prime}
and let
G_{i}^{\prime\prime}
be the union of all components of
G_{i}^{\prime}-S^{\prime}
that contain a vertex of
V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime\prime})
.
Notice that for each
i\in[\ell+1]
, both
V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime\prime})
separates
V(\Omega)
from
V(G_{i}^{\prime\prime})
in
G^{\prime}-S^{\prime}
.

Since each district
(G_{i}-S,\Omega_{i})
with
i\in[\ell+1]\setminus I_{2}
has a vortex-free rendition in a disk, this remains true for
(G_{i}^{\prime\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime\prime})
.

Let
\{(H_{i},\Omega_{i})\}_{i\in[\ell+1]}
be the areas of
\mathfrak{M}
.
Further, let
\{(H_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})\}_{i\in[\ell+1]}
be the family of
C_{s-s^{\prime}(\ell+1)}^{i}
-societies in the restrictions of
\rho_{i}
to
H_{i}
for each
i\in[\ell+1]
.
From
Definition

12.1
we know that the society
(H-\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]}(H_{i}-V(\Omega_{i})),\Omega)
has a vortex-free rendition
\rho^{*}
in a disk
\Delta
in which
\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]}V(\Omega_{i})\subseteq N(\rho^{*})
, such that
\rho^{*}
is a vortex-free rendition of
(H-\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]}(H_{i}-V(\Omega_{i})),\Omega_{i})
in a disk for each
i\in[\ell+1]
.
Using the renditions
\rho_{i}
for all
i\in[\ell+1]
, we can extend
\rho^{*}
to a rendition
\rho^{**}
of
(H-\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]}(H_{i}^{\prime}-V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})),\Omega)
in
\Delta
in which
\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]}V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})\subseteq N(\rho^{**})
, such that
\rho^{**}
is a vortex-free rendition of
(H-\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]}(H_{i}^{\prime}-V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})),\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
in a disk for each
i\in[\ell+1]
.

Let
H^{*}\coloneqq(H_{i}^{\prime}-V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime\prime}))\cup\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]\setminus I_{2}}G_{i}^{\prime\prime})-S^{\prime}
and set
X\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]}(V(G_{i})\setminus(V(G_{i}^{\prime\prime})\cup S^{\prime}))
.
Notice that every neighbour of
X
in
G
must belong to
S^{\prime}
by construction.
The rendition
\rho^{**}
can then be further augmented to a vortex-free rendition
\rho^{***}
of
(H^{*},\Omega)
in a
\Delta
in which
\bigcup_{i\in I_{2}}V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime\prime})\subseteq N(\rho^{***})
, such that
\rho^{***}
is a vortex-free rendition of
(H^{*},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
in a disk for each
i\in I_{2}
.
Finally, to construct the rendition
\rho^{\prime}
of
(G-S^{\prime},\Omega)
in a
\Delta
from the fourth item of our statement, we can now add each society
(G_{i}^{\prime\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime\prime})
with
i\in I_{2}
into
\rho^{***}
as a vortex and draw the graph
G[X]
in the interior of an arbitrary cell.
Since
|I_{2}|<\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)
, the rendition
\rho^{\prime}
has breadth at most
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)-1
and depth at most
d
.
The extended
k
-surface wall we seek is then recovered by applying
Lemma

13.2
to the collection
\{(H_{i},\Omega_{i})\}_{i\in I_{2}}
.

We continue with the case where we find a
V(\Omega^{\prime}_{i_{1}})
-
V(\Omega^{\prime}_{i_{2}})
-linkage
\mathcal{P}_{1}
of order
\mathsf{aux\text{-}link}(t,s,p,q)
in

for some

.
Set
J\coloneqq[\ell+1]\setminus\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
.

In this case we return the set

and all previously found sets

to the graph.
We may now apply
lemma

14.3
to
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and the societies
(G_{i_{1}}^{\prime},\Omega_{i_{1}}^{\prime})
,
(G_{i_{2}}^{\prime},\Omega_{i_{2}}^{\prime})

together with their corresponding paddings as provided by

.
As a result, we obtain a connecting linkage
\mathcal{P}_{2}
of order
(97t^{3}(p(q+1)+41))^{2}+8s+1
such that
\mathcal{P}_{2}
is orthogonal to the nests

.

Next, for each

We then apply

This results in two linkages

Moreover,

returns a planar transaction

in
(G^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime})
of order

\displaystyle\left\lfloor\frac{p}{2}\right\rfloor-k(\ell+1)-3\cdot\big(s\cdot((97t^{3}(q+40))^{2}+2)+8s+1\big)

\displaystyle\geq s(q+1)+1+8t^{3}

such that

separates

and

in

.
Additionally, we have that

,

Notice that

shares at most its endpoints with the graph

.
Indeed, we may now reunite

and

on

.
Moreover, every path from

.

At this point we have almost reached our goal.
The transaction
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
is already fully contained in the vortex-free rendition of

and therefore its strip society is flat and isolated in

.
Our next goal is to replace both

and

with transactions that are orthogonal to most of the nest of

.
This requires some amount of set up.

From here on we consider
\rho^{\prime\prime}
to be the vortex-free rendition of

into the disk
\Delta
as induced by

.

Let

Claim 14.7.1
.

Claim 14.7.2
.

There exist
\mathcal{F}_{i,j}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{3}\setminus\bigcup_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\in[2]}\mathcal{S}_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}
such that the minimal subpath

Proof of

:

For

be the endpoint of

that belongs to

.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{4}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}_{3}\setminus\bigcup_{i,j\in[2]}\big(\mathcal{F}_{i,j}\cup\mathcal{S}_{i,j}\big)
.

Next, for each

Consider the nest

.
Therefore, we may now obtain a transaction

of order at least

such that

.

Next, we call upon

3.3
to find a monotone transaction

.
With

.
This results either in a

.
We may assume to find the set

and the transaction
\mathcal{P}_{8}
.

We discard at most

many paths from
\mathcal{Q}_{1}

.

Suppose the

-strip in

.
Since

is disjoint from

and a transaction in

.
As

on

this is a contradiction to the strip society of
\mathcal{P}_{8}
being isolated in

.

Next, let

and

-strip in

.
By the discussion above we have that
N_{3}
and
N_{4}
are disjoint from the paths in
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
.
We now apply
lemma

10.6
to
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
and obtain a planar transaction
\mathcal{Q}_{3}
of order
q+2
which is orthogonal to
\mathcal{N}_{2}\coloneqq\mathcal{N}_{1}\setminus\{C_{2},\dots,C_{8t^{3}+2}\}
.
Similar to before, the only way how
\mathcal{Q}_{3}
can intersect
\mathcal{P}_{8}
is via the paths
N_{3}
and
N_{4}
.
Moreover, if some path of
\mathcal{Q}_{3}
contains a vertex of
N_{i}
for some
i\in[2]
then it must contain the entire path
N_{i}
.
Hence, by removing at most two paths from
\mathcal{Q}_{3}
we obtain a planar transaction
\mathcal{Q}_{4}
of order at least
q
which is orthogonal to
\mathcal{N}_{2}
.
Notice that the strip society of
\mathcal{Q}_{4}
in
(G^{\prime\prime}-A,\Omega^{\prime\prime})
is flat and isolated.

We then apply
proposition

7.2
to the nest
\mathcal{N}_{2}
and the transaction
\mathcal{P}_{8}
in
\rho^{\prime\prime\prime}
together with the two boundary paths of
\mathcal{Q}_{4}
.
In case
\mathcal{P}_{8}
is a crosscap transaction, the proof, at this point, is complete.
Otherwise,
\mathcal{P}_{8}
is planar.
In this case notice that we have now found a vortex-free rendition
\rho^{\star}
of the union of the outer graph of
C_{8t^{3}+9}
together with the
\mathcal{P}_{8}
-strip in
(G^{\prime\prime}-A,\Omega^{\prime\prime})
and the
\mathcal{Q}_{4}
-strip in
(G^{\prime\prime}-A,\Omega)
.
Hence, we have found an orthogonal and flat handle transaction of the required order and our proof is complete.
∎

14.2
Building a transaction mesh and updating the
\mathfrak{M}
-tree

For our proof of
Theorem

14.1
we will need a tool that lets us extend the structure of a transaction mesh for one more iteration.
These tools must be applicable whenever we cannot apply our results from
Section

14.1
, which means that for this section we can generally assume that our transaction meshes have a district that does not have bounded depth.
To allow us to integrate the transactions we can find there, we will need to do a lot of clean-up work.
This is one of the most technical parts of our proof as we will need to interact with the details in
Definition

12.1
and
Definition

12.14
.
In addition to this, we have finally reached the point at which we need
Theorem

11.5
.

We start with a lemma that is simple in spirit, but has a very complicated statement.
Essentially we want to ensure that, if we have some district with sufficient depth, we can either find a
K_{t}
-minor, a flat crosscap transaction on the original society, or we can find a planar, flat transaction which we can integrate into our transaction mesh.
We delay the integration into the transaction mesh to the lemma following this one.
The third item can best be understood by directly comparing it to the items
M2
to
M5
of
Definition

12.1
.
The proof of this lemma brings together most of the lemmas and theorems we have proven up to this point.

Lemma 14.8
.

There exist polynomial functions
\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:freeflattransactionorprotectedcross}}\colon\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,

We now show how to use the transaction we find in the third option of
Lemma

14.8
to extend our existing transaction mesh by one more transaction.
However, simply building this new transaction mesh is not enough, we will prove here that we can sensibly extend the
\mathfrak{M}
-tree of the previous transaction mesh.
This should finally shed some light into the origin and purpose of the various options found in
Definition

12.14
and in particular, give some intuition as to what the signature of a transaction mesh is tracking.
We will go into much more detail regarding
\mathfrak{M}
-trees and their signatures in the next subsection.

Lemma 14.9
.

There exists polynomial functions
\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{link}}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
,
\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{6}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
, and
\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{8}\rightarrow\mathbb{N}
, such that the following holds.

Let
t,s,s^{\prime},a,a^{\prime},p,q,k,z^{\prime},\ell
be non-negative integers
\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,z^{\prime},a^{\prime},s^{\prime},\ell,k)\leq s
,
8k+2a^{\prime}+8+s(q-1)\leq\nicefrac{{p}}{{4}}
,
z^{\prime}\leq\ell
, and
\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{link}}_{\ref{lem:freeflattransactionorprotectedcross}}(t,z^{\prime},k)\leq a
.

Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with a cylindrical rendition
\rho
in a disk
\Delta
, a cozy nest
\mathcal{C}=\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{s}\}
of order
s
in
\rho
around the vortex
c_{0}
, and a padded
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}
with the
k
-signature
\bar{x}=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})
, such that
x_{2}\leq z^{\prime}
and the union of the apices for the transactions in
\mathfrak{P}
have order at most
a^{\prime}
.
Further, let
\{(G_{i},\Omega_{i})\}_{i\in[\ell+1]}
be the districts of
\mathfrak{M}
with cylindrical renditions
\rho_{i}
in disks and an
(s-(s^{\prime}\ell+1),\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-(s^{\prime}\ell+1),\ell_{i})
-padding
(\mathcal{C}_{i}=\{C_{1}^{i},\ldots,C_{s-(s^{\prime}\ell+1)}^{i}\},\mathfrak{P}_{i})
with the inner nest
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime}=\{C_{1}^{i*},\ldots,C_{s-(s^{\prime}\ell+1)}^{i*}\}
.
Let
i\in[\ell+1]
be chosen such that
(G_{i}^{\prime}-Z,\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
has depth at least
\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,s,q,p,a^{\prime},\ell,z^{\prime},k)
, where
(G_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
is the
C_{s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1))}^{i*}
-society of
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
.
Finally, let
G^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]}G_{i}
and let
(G^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime})
be the
C_{s-s^{\prime}\ell}
-society in
\rho
.

There there exists a set
A\subseteq V(G^{\prime\prime})
, and

i)

a
K_{t}
-minor model in
G
controlled by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct cycles from
\mathcal{C}
,

ii)

a flat crosscap transaction
\mathcal{P}
of order
q
in
(G^{\prime\prime}-A,\Omega^{\prime\prime})
, with
|A|\leq\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{lem:freeflattransactionorprotectedcross}}(t,a^{\prime})
and a nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
\rho
of order
s-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:freeflattransactionorprotectedcross}}(t)
around
c_{0}
to which
\mathcal{P}
is orthogonal, or

iii)

we have
A\subseteq G^{\prime}
with
|A|\leq\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{link}}_{\ref{lem:freeflattransactionorprotectedcross}}(t,z^{\prime},k)
, there exists a transaction
\mathcal{P}
of order
p-2\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,k)
in
(G_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
with co-conspirators
\mathcal{W}
(on level 3), and a padded
(s-(\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,k)+s^{\prime}),s^{\prime},a,p-2\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,k),\ell+1)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=(H^{\prime},\Omega,\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\mathfrak{P}\cup\{\mathcal{P}_{\ell+1}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}\})
with the
k
-signature
\bar{y}
, such that
\bar{y}\geq\bar{x}
.

In particular, the
K_{t}
-minor model, the set
A
, the transaction
\mathcal{P}
, the co-conspirators
\mathcal{W}
, and the transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
can each be found in time
\mathbf{poly}(t+p+s^{\prime}+\ell+p^{\prime})|E(G)||V(G)|^{2}
.

Proof.

We give the following estimates for the functions in the statement.
Let
p^{\prime}\coloneqq p-2\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,k)
.

\displaystyle\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,k)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \max(8t^{3}+10,2k+12)

\displaystyle\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{link}}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,z^{\prime},k)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{link}}_{\ref{lem:freeflattransactionorprotectedcross}}(t,z^{\prime}(3k+2))

\displaystyle\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,z^{\prime},a^{\prime},s^{\prime},\ell,k)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,k)+\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,z^{\prime}(3k+2),a^{\prime},s^{\prime},\ell)

\displaystyle\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,s,q,p,a^{\prime},\ell,z^{\prime},k)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{depth}_{\ref{lem:freeflattransactionorprotectedcross}}(t,s,q,p^{\prime},\ell,z^{\prime}(3k+2))

We again provide some estimates for the order of some of these functions, using the estimates we derived in the proof of
Lemma

14.8
.
Two simpler estimates are for
\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,z^{\prime},a^{\prime},s^{\prime},\ell,k)\in\mathbf{O}(\max(t^{3},k)+s^{\prime}\ell+z^{\prime}k+a^{\prime})
and for
\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{link}}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,z^{\prime},k)\in\mathbf{O}(t^{3}+z^{\prime}k)
.
The most complex estimate yields
\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,s,q,p^{\prime},\ell,z^{\prime},k)\in\mathbf{O}(t^{9}pqs^{2}\ell^{2}+t^{6}z^{\prime}kpqs^{2}\ell^{2})
, using the fact that
p^{\prime}\leq p
.

Let
\mathfrak{T}=(T,\mathfrak{M},\phi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Z}=\{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{\ell}\},\mathcal{F},\mathfrak{L})
be the
\mathfrak{M}
-tree of precision
k
that witnesses the signature
\bar{x}
of
\mathfrak{M}
and let
\mathfrak{P}=\{\mathcal{P}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell}\}
.
Furthermore, let
\mathsf{z}\in[\ell]
be the maximal integer such that
A_{i}=\emptyset
for all
i\in[(\ell+1)-\mathsf{z},\ell]
.
We let
Z\coloneqq\bigcup_{j=(\ell+1)-\mathsf{z}}^{\ell}B_{\mathfrak{T}}(Z_{i})
.
Note that according to
T5
we have
Z\subseteq V(G^{\prime})\setminus V(H)
and furthermore, we have
|Z|\leq(3k+2)z^{\prime}
, due to
x_{3}\leq z^{\prime}
.

Let
(G_{j}^{\prime},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
be the
C_{s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1))}^{j}
-society of the district
(G_{j},\Omega_{j})
with
j\in[\ell+1]
which has depth at least
\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,s,q,p^{\prime},\ell,z^{\prime},k)
.
Note that we use the index
j
here in reference to the index
j_{i}
that first appears in
M1
of
Definition

12.1
.
In the context of this proof our
j
will correspond to
j_{\ell+1}
in the construction of our new transaction mesh.
We now apply
Lemma

14.8
at
(G_{j},\Omega_{j})
using our
Z
.

Of course the first two options of that statement correspond directly to the first two options of the present statement and thus, if we reach either of them, we are done.
Otherwise, we find the planar, isolated, flat transaction
\mathcal{P}
and the set
A\subseteq V(G^{\prime})
with
|A|\leq\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{link}}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,z^{\prime},k)
which together have the properties described in the third option of the statement of
Lemma

14.8
.
Let
\mathcal{P}=\{P_{1},\ldots,P_{p^{\prime}}\}
and let
\mathcal{C}=\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{s}\}
.

Our goal will be to add
\mathcal{P}_{\ell+1}\coloneq\mathcal{P}
to
\mathfrak{M}
, make some modifications to
\mathfrak{M}
and then verify the conditions set out in
Definition

12.1
, to show that this yields a new transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
.
We will also have to confirm that
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
remains padded.
Following this, we need to update the signature and analyse the
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
-tree to confirm the third point of our statement.

Our analysis will be split into two cases, depending on whether or not
A
is empty.
Nonetheless, the construction of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
and the verification of its properties is largely the same.
Thus we begin with discussing the properties of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
we can guarantee in both cases.

We let
a^{\prime\prime}\in\{10,|A|+10\}
and for now let
H^{\prime}
be a graph whose definition we specify later on.
Both of these objects will be made concrete within the two cases we later consider.
Let
\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}
be derived from
\mathfrak{P}
by iteratively removing the boundary paths of each
\mathcal{P}\in\mathfrak{P}
for
a^{\prime\prime}
iterations.
Our first goal is to show that in most cases
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=(H^{\prime},\Omega,\mathcal{C}\setminus\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{a^{\prime\prime}}\},\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\cup\{\mathcal{P}_{\ell+1}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}\})
is the
(s-(a^{\prime\prime}+s^{\prime}),s^{\prime},a,p-2a^{\prime\prime},\ell+1)
-transaction mesh we are after.

To verify this, we need to check the properties demanded of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
in
Definition

12.1
and
Definition

12.12
.
Let
\{(H_{i},\Omega_{i})\}_{i\in[\ell+1]}
be the areas of
\mathfrak{M}
with the
(s-(s^{\prime}\ell+1),\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}-(s^{\prime}\ell+1)\rfloor,\ell_{i})
-padding
(\mathcal{C}_{i}=\{C_{1}^{i},\ldots,C_{s-(s^{\prime}\ell+1)}^{i}\},\mathfrak{P}_{i}=\{\mathcal{P}_{1}^{i},\ldots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell_{i}}^{i}\})
and for all
j\in[\ell_{i}]
let
\mathcal{P}_{j}^{i}=\{P_{1}^{j,i},\ldots,P_{\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}-(s^{\prime}\ell+1)\rfloor}^{j,i}\}
.
For all
i\in[\ell+1]\setminus\{j\}
, we can construct the new area
(H_{i}^{\ell+1},\Omega^{\ell+1})
and set
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\ell+1}=\{C_{a^{\prime\prime}+1}^{i},\ldots,C_{s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1)}^{i}\}
, largely following the instructions laid out in
M1
of
Definition

12.1
.
This allows us to let the new padding be defined by taking the
s^{\prime}
-narrowing of
(\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\ell+1},\{\{P_{a^{\prime\prime}+1}^{1,i},\ldots,P_{s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1)}^{1,i}\},\ldots,\{P_{a^{\prime\prime}+1}^{\ell_{i},i},\ldots,P_{s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1)}^{\ell_{i},i}\}\})
(see
Lemma

12.9
).
Thus for
i\in[\ell+1]\setminus\{j\}
we have confirmed that the area
(H_{i}^{\ell+1},\Omega^{\ell+1})
is padded in accordance with
Definition

12.12
.

Let
(H_{j}^{\prime},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
be the
C_{s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1))}^{j}
-society of
(H_{j},\Omega_{j})
in the restriction of
\rho_{j}
to
H_{j}
.
The transaction
\mathcal{P}
is planar and exposed in
(H_{j}^{\prime}\cup\bigcup\mathcal{P},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
, as well as being disjoint from
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\ell+1}
for all
i\in[\ell+1]\setminus\{j\}
, according to point iii)d) of
Lemma

14.8
.
This confirms
M2
of
Definition

12.1
.

For
M3
, we can place
A
into the role of
A_{\ell+1}
, note that the graph
G^{\prime}
defined there corresponds to the component of
G^{\prime}
defined in our statement that contains
H_{j}^{\prime}
.
We know that the strip-society
(U_{j},\Psi_{j})
of
\mathcal{P}
in
(G^{\prime},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
has a vortex-free rendition
\rho_{j}^{S}
thanks to point iii)b) of
Lemma

14.8
.
The rendition
\tau_{j}
of
(H_{j}^{\prime}\cup U_{j},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
in a disk
\Delta_{j}
demanded in the remainder of
M3
is confirmed the exist by point iii)c) of
Lemma

14.8
.
We restate the properties of
\tau_{j}
in the context of the objects we defined here for the convenience of the reader.
The rendition
\tau_{j}
agrees with
\rho_{j}^{S}
on
U_{j}
and agrees with
\rho_{j}
on the union of
C_{8}^{i*}
and the outer graph of
C_{8}^{i*}
in
\rho_{j}
.
Furthermore, the trace in
\tau_{j}
of each path in
\mathcal{P}
separates the unique pair of vortices
v_{1},v_{2}
of
\tau_{j}
in
\Delta_{j}
.

The items
M4
and
M5
of
Definition

12.1
follow directly from the properties of
\mathcal{P}
and
A
laid out in
Lemma

14.8
.
Thus we are left with having to confirm
M6
of
Definition

12.1
and having to construct a nice padding for the resulting pair of areas.

We can define the new areas
(H_{j}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{j}^{\ell+1}),(H_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1})
in a straightforward way by using the properties of
\tau_{j}
and following the instructions laid out in i), ii), and iii) of
M6
, where we let
Q_{L}\coloneqq P_{\lfloor\nicefrac{{p^{\prime}}}{{2}}\rfloor}
, and
Q_{R}\coloneqq P_{\lceil\nicefrac{{(p^{\prime}+1)}}{{2}}\rceil}
.
Let
\rho_{L}
be the restriction of
\tau_{j}
to
H_{j}^{\ell+1}
and let
\rho_{R}
be the restriction of
H_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1}
.
We assume that
v_{1},v_{2}
are named such that
\rho_{L}
is a cylindrical rendition of
(H_{j}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{j}^{\ell+1})
in a disk around
v_{1}
and
\rho_{R}
is a cylindrical rendition of
(H_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1})
in a disk around
v_{2}
.
To construct the new nest for
(H_{j}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{j}^{\ell+1})
, for each
i\in[a^{\prime\prime}+1,s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1)]
, we let
C_{i}^{j,\ell+1}
be cycle whose
v_{1}
-disk in
\rho_{L}
is inclusion-wise maximal with respect to the set of cycles in
C_{i}^{j}\cup P_{(\lfloor\nicefrac{{p^{\prime}}}{{2}}\rfloor+i)-s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+2)}
.
We let
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\ell+1*}=\{C_{i}^{j,\ell+1}~\!\colon\!~i\in[a^{\prime\prime}+1,s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1)]\}
and define
\mathcal{C}_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1*}
analogous for
(H_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1})
and
\rho_{R}
.
This allows us to simply apply
Lemma

8.4
to make the nests
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\ell+1*},\mathcal{C}_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1*}
cozy, yielding two nests
\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\ell+1},\mathcal{C}_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1}
and confirming the remaining parts of point iii) in
M6
.

To construct the appropriate padding for these two areas we first take the
s^{\prime}
-narrowing
(\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\ell+1\star},\{\{P_{a^{\prime\prime}+1}^{1,j},\ldots,P_{s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1)}^{1,j}\},\ldots,\{P_{a^{\prime\prime}+1}^{\ell_{j},j},\ldots,P_{s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1)}^{\ell_{j},j}\}\})
of the
(s-(s^{\prime}\ell+1),\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}-(s^{\prime}\ell+1)\rfloor,\ell_{i})
-padding of
(H_{j}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{j}^{\ell+1})
.
The
(s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1)-a^{\prime\prime},\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1-a^{\prime\prime})\rfloor,\ell_{j}^{*})
-padding
(\mathcal{C}_{j}^{\ell+1},\mathfrak{P}_{j}^{\ell+1})
for
(H_{j}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{j}^{\ell+1})
is then formed by letting, for each maximal segment
S
of
\Omega_{j}^{\ell+1}
with
S\subseteq V(P_{s-s^{\prime}(\ell+1)}^{i,j}
for some
i\in[\ell_{j}]
,
\mathcal{P}_{i}^{j,\ell+1}
be a transaction on
V(\Omega_{j}^{\ell+1}
in
\bigcup_{h=a^{\prime\prime}+1}^{s-s^{\prime}(\ell+1)-1}P_{h}^{i,j}
and adding these transactions to
\mathfrak{P}_{j}^{\ell+1}
.
These exists thanks to the fact that the paths involved in the construction are grounded in
\rho_{L}
.
We note in particular that using the same arguments as in the proof of
Lemma

12.9
, one can see that there exist at most
\ell_{j}
such segments
S
and thus
\ell_{j}^{*}\leq\ell_{j}+1
.
Analogous constructions yield an
(s-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1)-a^{\prime\prime},\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}-(s^{\prime}(\ell+1)+1-a^{\prime\prime})\rfloor,\ell_{\ell+2}^{*})
-padding for
(H_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1})
.
This confirms that our new transaction mesh is padded.

For the parts that need divergent analysis, we start with the easier of the two cases.

Non-empty apex set:

Suppose that
A\neq\emptyset
.
We let
a^{\prime\prime}=|A|+10
in this case.
Let
(U_{j},\Psi_{j})
be the
\mathcal{P}
-society in
(G_{j}^{\prime}-(A\cup Z),\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
.
Then according to
Lemma

14.8
iii)f)2), if we let
(U_{j}^{\prime},\Psi_{j}^{\prime})
be the
\{Q_{L},Q_{R}\}
-society in
(U_{j},\Psi_{j})
, the society
(G_{j}^{\prime}[V(U_{j}^{\prime})\cup A\cup Z],\Psi_{j}^{\prime})
has a cross and
A\subseteq V(G^{\prime})
.

We let
H^{\prime}
be the union of
U_{j}
and
H-A
.
The society
(H^{\prime},\Omega)
then has a vortex-free rendition since
(H,\Omega)
has a vortex-free rendition according to
Definition

12.1
and
(H_{j}^{\prime}\cup U_{j},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
has a vortex-free rendition, as we just noted.
Furthermore, using
\tau_{j}
and the special rendition
\rho^{\dagger}
from
Definition

12.1
for
\mathfrak{M}
, it is easy to derive the vortex-free rendition
\rho^{\dagger\dagger}
of the society
(H^{\prime}-\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+2]}(H_{i}^{\ell+1}-V(\Omega_{i}^{\ell+1})),\Omega)
in a disk in which
\bigcup_{i\in[\ell+1]}V(\Omega_{i}^{\ell+1})\subseteq N(\rho^{\dagger\dagger})
, such that for each
i\in[\ell+1]
,
\rho^{\dagger\dagger}
is a vortex-free rendition of
(H^{\prime}-\bigcup_{j\in[\ell+1]\setminus\{i\}}(H_{j}^{\ell+1}-V(\Omega_{j}^{\ell+1})),\Omega_{i}^{\ell+1})
in a disk.
We then let
A_{\ell+1}\coloneqq A\cup Z
, which are the apices associated with
\mathcal{P}
.
As a result we have confirmed that
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
is a padded
(s-(a^{\prime\prime}+s^{\prime}),s^{\prime},a,p-2a^{\prime\prime},\ell+1)
-transaction mesh, as desired.

We now turn our attention towards the the
\mathfrak{M}
-tree
\mathfrak{T}=(T,\mathfrak{M},\phi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{F},\mathfrak{L})
of precision
k
.
Here our construction for the new
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
-tree
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
is fairly straightforward.
In particular, we let
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}=(T^{\prime},\mathfrak{M}^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\mathcal{A}\cup\{A_{\ell+1}\},\mathcal{Z}\cup\{Z_{\ell+1}\coloneqq\emptyset\},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathfrak{L}\cup\{\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}\coloneqq\emptyset\})
, where
T^{\prime}
and
\phi^{\prime}
are derived from
T
and
\phi
in the way described by
T1
of
Definition

12.14
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is similarly derived directly via the instructions laid out in
T8
of
Definition

12.14
.
Note that
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
also has precision
k
.
From the definition of the
k
-signature
\bar{y}
of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
given in
Definition

12.14
, we can now easily derive the fact that
\bar{y}\geq\bar{x}
, since
A_{\ell+1}\neq\emptyset
.
This concludes the analysis of this case.

Empty apex set:

Suppose instead that
A=\emptyset
.
We let
a^{\prime\prime}=10
.
Note that
M8
of
Definition

12.1
holds trivially, since
A=\emptyset
.
Thus, using
A_{\ell+1}=\emptyset
, and
H^{\prime}
as the union of
H
and
U_{j}
, where
(U_{j},\Psi_{j})
is the
\mathcal{P}
-society in
(G_{j}^{\prime},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
, we confirm that
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
is a
(s-(a^{\prime\prime}+s^{\prime}),s^{\prime},a,p-2a^{\prime\prime},\ell+1)
-transaction mesh using analogous arguments to the previous case.
We will have to replace
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
in the last subcase we consider to actually build a proper
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
-tree.
But for the other subcases
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
will suffice.

We now again have to consider how the new
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
-tree
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}
can be constructed.
For this purpose, we can once more extend
T
and
\phi
of the
\mathfrak{M}
-tree
\mathfrak{T}=(T,\mathfrak{M},\phi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{F},\mathfrak{L})
of precision
k
in a straightforward fashion to
T^{\prime}
and
\phi^{\prime}
in the way described by
T1
of
Definition

12.14
.
The remainder of our arguments now focuses on how the two new districts
(G_{j}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{j}^{\ell+1}),(G_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1})
, respectively corresponding to
(H_{j}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{j}^{\ell+1}),(H_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1},\Omega_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1})
, in
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
are connected to each other in
G^{*}\coloneqq\bigcup G_{i}^{*}-Z
, where
\{(G_{i}^{*},\Omega_{i}^{*})\}_{i\in[\ell+2]}
are the districts of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
with
G_{j}^{*}=G_{j}^{\ell+1}
and
G_{\ell+2}^{*}=G_{\ell+2}^{\ell+1}
.

First, suppose that there exists a
V(\Omega_{j}^{*})
-
V(\Omega_{\ell+2}^{*})
-linkage
\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}
of order
k+1
in
G^{*}
.
In this case we can let
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}=(T^{\prime},\mathfrak{M}^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\mathcal{A}\cup\{A_{\ell+1}\},\mathcal{Z}\cup\{Z_{\ell+1}\coloneqq\emptyset\},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathfrak{L}\cup\{\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}\})
, where
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is constructed via the instructions laid out in
T8
of
Definition

12.14
.
We note that the order of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
cannot have changed when compared to
\mathcal{F}
, as we only moved
s^{\prime}
cycles inwards, leaving each area connected, since
s\geq\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,z^{\prime},a^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime},\ell)
in each area and we did not delete any vertices otherwise.
Thus, if
\bar{y}
is the
k
-signature of
\mathfrak{T}
, then we have
\bar{y}>\bar{x}
, as the non-empty linkage
\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}
was added to
\mathfrak{L}
.

Thus, we may instead suppose that there exists a
V(\Omega_{j}^{*})
-
V(\Omega_{\ell+2}^{*})
-separator
Z^{\prime}
in
G^{*}
.
We note that
V(G^{*})\cap V(H)=\emptyset
and we can choose
Z^{\prime}
to be minimal.
The case in which
Z^{\prime}=\emptyset
has to be considered separately.
Here, we let
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}=(T^{\prime},\mathfrak{M}^{\prime},\phi^{\prime},\mathcal{A}\cup\{A_{\ell+1}\},\mathcal{Z}\cup\{Z_{\ell+1}\coloneqq\emptyset\},\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathfrak{L}\cup\{\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}\coloneqq\emptyset\})
, where
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is constructed via the instructions laid out in
T8
of
Definition

12.14
.
The number of sets in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
cannot decrease since the strip of
\mathcal{P}
may split a family into two, but it does so within a single district and thus produces a new district for the family.
In fact, if the number of families stays the same, then, due to the presence of the co-conspirators for
\mathcal{P}
, this results in us finding more districts that host crosses.
Thus, if
\bar{y}
is the
k
-signature of
\mathfrak{T}
, we have we have
\bar{y}>\bar{x}
.

We may therefore move on to the case in which
Z^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
.
This means we mainly need to confirm that
T5
of
Definition

12.14
holds.
Consider a partitioning
\mathcal{F}^{*}
of
[\ell+2]
into as few sets as possible such that for all
F\in\mathcal{F}^{*}
with
|F|\geq 2
there exists a
V(\Omega_{h}^{\ell+1})
-
