V(\Omega_{h^{\prime}}^{\ell+1})
-path in
G^{*}-Z^{\prime}
for all distinct
h,h^{\prime}\in F
.
We then let
F_{j},F_{\ell+2}\in\mathcal{F}^{*}
be the sets with
j\in F_{j}
and
\ell+2\in F_{\ell+2}
, which must be distinct as
Z^{\prime}
is a
V(\Omega_{h}^{\ell+1})
-
V(\Omega_{h^{\prime}}^{\ell+1})
-separator in
G^{*}
.
Let
J^{*}
be the union of
\bigcup_{i\in F_{j}\cup F_{\ell+2}}V(\Omega_{i}^{\ell+1})
and all
\bigcup_{i\in F_{j}\cup F_{\ell+2}}V(\Omega_{i}^{\ell+1})
-bridges in
G^{*}-Z^{\prime}
.
Further, let
w\in Z^{\prime}
be arbitrary and let
J_{w}\coloneqq G^{*}[V(J^{*})\cup\{w\}]
.

Note that due to the minimality of
Z^{\prime}
, there exists a
V(\Omega_{j}^{*})
-
V(\Omega_{\ell+2}^{*})
-path in
J_{w}
.
In particular, every
V(\Omega_{j}^{*})
-
V(\Omega_{\ell+2}^{*})
-path in
J_{w}
must use
w
.
Accordingly, there exists a separation
(A_{j},A_{\ell+2})
in
J_{w}
with
V(\Omega_{j}^{*})\subseteq A_{j}
,
V(\Omega_{\ell+2}^{*})\subseteq A_{\ell+2}
, and
A_{j}\cap A_{\ell+2}=\{w\}
.
We let
W_{i}=\{V(\Omega_{h}^{\ell+1}~\!\colon\!~h\in F_{i}\}
for both
i\in\{j,\ell+2\}
and note that
W_{i}\subseteq V(J_{w}[A_{i}])
.
Since
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
is a
(s-a^{\prime\prime},s^{\prime},a,p-2a^{\prime\prime},\ell+1)
-transaction mesh, there exist radial linkages
\mathcal{R}_{i}
of order
\nicefrac{{p}}{{4}}\geq 2k+2
for
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\ell+1}
for all
i\in[\ell+2]
, according to
Observation

12.11
.

For both
i\in\{j,\ell+2\}
, we apply
Proposition

12.13
in
J_{w}[A_{i}]
to try to separate
\{w\}
and
W_{i}
, with
\{w\}
in the role of
X
in the statement.
This finds a separation
(S_{i},T_{i})
such that
W_{i}\subseteq T_{i}
and
\{w\}\subseteq S_{i}
.
Let
S^{\prime}_{i}
be
S_{i}\cap T_{i}
.
We claim that
S^{\prime}
is disjoint from the union of
C^{h}_{2k+a^{\prime\prime}+3}
and the outer graph of
C^{h}_{2k+a^{\prime\prime}+3}
in
\rho_{h}^{\prime}
for all
h\in[\ell+2]
.
Suppose this is not the case and thus, due to
\rho_{h}^{\prime}
being a cylindrical rendition, there exists some
v\in V(C_{2k+a^{\prime\prime}+3}^{h})\cap S^{\prime}
.
However, this also implies that for each
h^{\prime}\in[a^{\prime\prime}+1,\ldots,2k+a^{\prime\prime}+3]
the set
V(C_{h^{\prime}}^{h})\cap S^{\prime}
is non-empty, since we could otherwise get to
W_{i}
via
\mathcal{R}_{h}
which clearly contradicts the fact that
|S^{\prime}|\leq k
.
This in particular implies that
S^{\prime}\not\subseteq W_{i}
.

Thus
Proposition

12.13
tells us that
|S_{i}\cap T_{i}|=k
and there exists an
(S_{i}\cap T_{i})
-
W_{i}
-linkage
\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\prime}
of order
k+1
in
J_{w}[T_{i}]
.
Thus,
(S,T)
with
S=S_{j}\cup S_{\ell+2}
and
T=T_{j}\cup T_{\ell+2}
, is a separation with
W_{i}\subseteq T
for both
i\in\{j,\ell+2\}
and
|S\cap T|\leq 2k+2
, such that
\mathcal{L}_{j}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}_{\ell+2}^{\prime}
is an
S
-
T
-linkage of order
2k+2
in
J_{w}[T]
.
We let
Z_{\ell+1}^{\prime}=Z^{\prime}\cup(S\cap T)
and note that
|Z^{\prime}|\leq 3k+2
.
In particular, our earlier argument for
S^{\prime}_{i}
also tells us that both
S
and
Z
must be disjoint from the union of
C^{i}_{2k+a^{\prime\prime}+3}
and the outer graph of
C^{i}_{2k+a^{\prime\prime}+3}
in
\rho_{i}^{\prime}
for all
i\in[\ell+2]
.

Thus, we can now define a new transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}^{*}=(H^{*},\Omega,\mathcal{C}\setminus\{C_{1},\ldots,C_{2k+a^{\prime\prime}+2}\},\mathfrak{P}^{\prime}\cup\{\mathcal{P}_{\ell+1}\coloneqq\mathcal{P}\})
, with
H^{*}
being
H-Z_{\ell+1}^{\prime}
, which inherits all necessary properties from
\mathfrak{M}^{*}
to ensure that it is a
(s-(2k+12+s^{\prime}),s^{\prime},a,p-2a^{\prime\prime},\ell+1)
-transaction mesh.
Finally, we can let
\mathfrak{T}^{\prime}=(T^{\prime},\mathfrak{M}^{*},\phi^{\prime},\mathcal{A}\cup\{A_{\ell+1}\},\mathcal{Z}\cup\{Z_{\ell+1}^{\prime}\},\mathcal{F}^{*},\mathfrak{L}\cup\{\mathcal{L}_{\ell+1}\coloneqq\emptyset\})
, where
\mathcal{F}^{*}
is a partitioning of
[\ell+1]
into as few sets as possible such that for all
F\in\mathcal{F}
with
|F|\geq 2
there exists a
V(\Omega_{i}^{*})
-
V(\Omega_{j}^{*})
-path in
\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell+1}G_{i}^{*}-(Z\cup Z_{\ell+1}^{\prime})
for all distinct
i,j\in F
, fitting the conditions laid out in
T8
of
Definition

12.14
.
Since we added a non-empty set to
\mathcal{Z}
, we conclude that
\bar{x}
is smaller than the
k
-signature
\bar{y}
of
\mathfrak{M}^{*}
, finishing our proof.
∎

14.3
Analysing signatures

One of the main problems we need to solve in relation to transaction meshes is that we cannot keep building them for too many iterations.
As a transaction mesh grows, we are slowly gathering apices that may destroy parts of the infrastructure of the transaction mesh and there is even an in-built loss of infrastructure that keeps gnawing away at our nest.
The goal of this subsection is therefore to show that for any given integer
t
there is a concrete number of iterations polynomial in
t
after which we must find a
K_{t}
-minor.
This allows us to later set up initial nest to be big enough to endure that many iterations.

We will be heavily relying on
Theorem

13.1
within this section and in particular, we will rely on the bounds for the sets presented in the theorem’s second option.
Thus we simply codify these as a value depending on
t
.

Definition 14.10
.

Let
t
be a positive integer.
We let

\mathsf{d}_{t}\coloneq 72t^{3}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)-144.

Note that
\mathsf{d}_{t}
is exactly the maximum number of elements contained in the union of the objects
S
and
I
defined in the second option of
Theorem

13.1
.

Our first – and by far the easiest lemma in this subsection – demonstrates nicely how exactly we plan to use
Theorem

13.1
.

Lemma 14.11
.

Let
t,s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell
be non-negative integers with
s\geq(2t+24)(8t^{3}+20)+s^{\prime}\ell
and
p\geq(64t^{3}-128)(2t+24)
.
Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with an
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P})
and let
(a^{\prime},z,f,h)
be the
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signature of
\mathfrak{M}
.

If
f>\mathsf{d}_{t}
the graph
G
contains a
K_{t}
-minor model controlled by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct cycles from
\mathcal{C}
.

Furthermore, there exists an algorithm that finds this
K_{t}
-minor model in time
\mathbf{O}(t^{4}|E(G)|)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{T}=(T,\mathfrak{M},\phi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{F},\mathfrak{L})
be the
\mathfrak{M}
-tree of precision
\mathsf{d}_{t}
that witnesses the signature of
\mathfrak{M}
.
Applying
Theorem

13.1
either yields a
K_{t}
-minor model with our desired properties, or two sets
S\subseteq V(G^{\prime})
and
I\subseteq[\ell+1]
with
|S|\leq 8t^{3}-16
and
|I|\leq 64t^{3}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)-128
with the properties laid out in the statement of
Theorem

13.1
.
Note that the set
S
can only separate at most
8t^{3}-16
families in
\mathcal{F}
into societies with vortex-free renditions and only
64t^{3}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)-128
of the remaining families can be covered by having a district included in
I
.
As
f>\mathsf{d}_{t}
, this leads to a contradiction to the existence of these sets.
∎

We next want to show that the parameter
h
in the signature of a transaction mesh cannot grow indefinitely.
For this purpose we show an intermediate lemma that argues that two sets
S
and
I
defined as in the second option of
Theorem

13.1
must be at least as large as
h
.

Lemma 14.12
.

Let
t,s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell
be non-negative integers.
Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with an
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P})
, let
(G_{1},\Omega_{1}),\ldots,(G_{\ell+1},\Omega_{\ell+1})
be the districts of
\mathfrak{M}
, and let
G^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell+1}G_{i}
.
Further, let
(a^{\prime},z,f,h)
be the
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signature of
\mathfrak{M}
.

Then for any pair of sets
S\subseteq V(G^{\prime})
and
I\subseteq[\ell+1]
such that each
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell+1]\}
-path in
G^{\prime}-S
is a
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in I\}
-path and each district
(G_{j},\Omega_{j})
with
j\in\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell+1]\setminus I\}
has a vortex-free rendition in a disk, we have
|S|+|I|\geq h
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{T}=(T,\mathfrak{M},\phi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{F},\mathfrak{L}=\{\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{\ell}\})
be the
\mathfrak{M}
-tree of precision
\mathsf{d}_{t}
that witnesses the signature of
\mathfrak{M}
and let
\mathfrak{P}=\{\mathcal{P}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell}\}
.

We will prove this statement by induction over
h
, noting that it is trivially true for
h=0
.
Let
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P}\setminus\{\mathcal{P}_{\ell}\})
be the
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell-1)
-transaction mesh in
(G,\Omega)
, which can be derived from
\mathfrak{M}
since
\ell\geq h\geq 1
, such that
(G_{1}^{\prime},\Omega_{1}^{\prime}),\ldots,(G_{\ell}^{\prime},\Omega_{\ell}^{\prime})
are the districts of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
.
For each
i\in[\ell]
, we let
\mathcal{C}=\{C_{1}^{i,\ell},\ldots,C_{s-(s^{\prime}i+1)}^{i,\ell}\}
be the district nest of
(G_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
.
Since
h\geq 1
, we know that the
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signature of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
is
(a^{\prime},z,f,h^{\prime})
with
h\geq h^{\prime}\geq h-1
.
If
h^{\prime}=h
, then our statement holds by induction.
Thus we may suppose that
h^{\prime}=h-1
.

Suppose that our lemma is false and somehow there exists a pair of sets
S\subseteq V(G^{\prime})
and
I\subseteq[\ell+1]
as in the statement with
|S|+|I|<h
.
As
h\geq 1
, we know that
\mathcal{L}_{\ell}
is a linkage of order
\mathsf{d}_{t}+1
that joins
V(\Omega_{i+1})
to
V(\Omega_{j_{i}})
for some
j_{i}\in[\ell]
.
In particular, since
\mathsf{d}_{t}+1>\mathsf{d}_{t}
, there exists a path in
\mathcal{L}_{\ell}
that is not hit by any vertex in
S
and thus, we have
i+1,j_{i}\in I
.

By definition of
\mathfrak{M}
and
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, the linkage
\mathcal{P}_{\ell}
is found in the district
(G_{j_{i}}^{\prime},\Omega_{j_{i}}^{\prime})
of
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
.
Let
J=I\setminus\{i+1\}
and
G^{\prime\prime}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell}G_{i}^{\prime}
.

Claim 14.12.1
.

Each
\{V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell]\}
-path in
G^{\prime\prime}-S
is a
\{V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})~\!\colon\!~i\in J\}
-path and each district
(G_{j}^{\prime},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
with
j\in\{V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell]\setminus J\}
has a vortex-free rendition in a disk.

Proof of
Claim

14.12.1
:

First, let us consider an arbitrary
j\in\{V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell]\setminus J\}
and note that
j\in\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell+1]\setminus I\}
.
Thus there already exists a vortex-free rendition of the
C_{s-s^{\prime}(i+1)}^{i,\ell}
-society of
(G_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
, which can easily be extended to also encompass the outer graph of
C_{s-s^{\prime}(i+1)}^{i,\ell}
.

Thus we may suppose that there somehow exists a
\{V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell]\}
-path in
G^{\prime\prime}-S
that has at least one endpoint outside of
\bigcup_{i\in J}V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
.
Suppose this endpoint is found in
j^{\prime}\in[\ell]\setminus J
.
Since
j_{i}\in J
and the areas of
\mathfrak{M}
and
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
have vortex-free renditions, this path contains a subpath that is a
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell]\}
-path and in particular a
\bigcup_{i\in I}V(\Omega_{i})
-
V(\Omega_{j^{\prime}})
-path.
This contradicts each
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell]\}
-path in
G^{\prime}-S
being a
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in I\}
-path, as
j^{\prime}\not\in I
.

\blacksquare

We note that
|J|=|I|-1
and thus we have
|S|+|J|<h-1
, since
|S|+|I|<h
.
However, since by
Claim

14.12.1
, the sets
S
and
J
perform as required in our statement, we should in fact have
|S|+|J|\geq h-1
according to our induction hypothesis.
This is absurd and completes our proof.
∎

Using this lemma it becomes easy to conclude that
h
cannot grow indefinitely without producing a large
K_{t}
-minor model for us.

Corollary 14.13
.

Let
t,s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell
be non-negative integers with
s\geq(2t+24)(8t^{3}+20)+s^{\prime}\ell
and
p\geq(64t^{3}-128)(2t+24)
.
Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with an
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P})
and let
(a^{\prime},z,f,h)
be the
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signature of
\mathfrak{M}
.

If
h>\mathsf{d}_{t}
the graph
G
contains a
K_{t}
-minor model controlled by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct cycles from
\mathcal{C}
.

Furthermore, there exists an algorithm that finds this
K_{t}
-minor model in time
\mathbf{O}(t^{4}|E(G)|)
.

Proof.

Let
(G_{1},\Omega_{1}),\ldots,(G_{\ell+1},\Omega_{\ell+1})
be the districts of
\mathfrak{M}
and let
G^{\prime}\coloneqq\bigcup_{i=1}^{\ell+1}G_{i}
.
According to
Lemma

14.12
, if
h\geq\mathsf{d}_{t}
, we know in particular that for any pair of sets
S\subseteq V(G^{\prime})
and
I\subseteq[\ell+1]
such that each
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell+1]\}
-path in
G^{\prime}-S
is a
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in I\}
-path and each district
(G_{j},\Omega_{j})
with
j\in\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell+1]\setminus I\}
has a vortex-free rendition in a disk, we have
|S|+|I|\geq h
.
However, as
h>\mathsf{d}_{t}
, this implies that we can find a
K_{t}
-minor model via
Theorem

13.1
as desired.
∎

Next, we take on the parameter
z
of the signature of a transaction mesh.
To see why this parameter should also be limited in its growth, we point to the fact that, similar to
h
,
z
guarantees the existence of a lot of connectivity between the new districts.
As the transaction mesh grows and whilst we are not encountering a cross that lets
a^{\prime}
grow, the parts of
G
that provide this connectivity are disjoint from each other by construction.
As a consequence, two sets
S
and
I
as in the second option of
Theorem

13.1
can again not cover all of the connecting infrastructure between the districts of our transaction mesh.
Whilst this is simple to claim, actually proving this is another matter.
This is without a doubt the most complex part of this subsection.

Lemma 14.14
.

Let
t,s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell
be non-negative integers with
s\geq(2t+24)(8t^{3}+20)+s^{\prime}\ell
and
p\geq(64t^{3}-128)(2t+24)
.
Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with an
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P})
and let
(a^{\prime},z,f,h)
be the
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signature of
\mathfrak{M}
.

If
z\geq\mathsf{d}_{t}
the graph
G
contains a
K_{t}
-minor model controlled by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct cycles from
\mathcal{C}
.
Furthermore, there exists an algorithm that finds this
K_{t}
-minor model in time
\mathbf{O}(t^{4}|E(G)|)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{T}=(T,\mathfrak{M},\phi,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Z}=\{Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{\ell}\},\mathcal{F},\mathfrak{L})
be the
\mathfrak{M}
-tree of precision
\mathsf{d}_{t}
that witnesses the signature of
\mathfrak{M}
and let
\mathfrak{P}=\{\mathcal{P}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell}\}
.
Furthermore, let
\mathsf{z}\in[\ell]
be the maximal integer such that
A_{i}=\emptyset
for all
i\in[(\ell+1)-\mathsf{z},\ell]
and note that since
z\geq\mathsf{d}_{t}
, we have
|[(\ell+1)-\mathsf{z},\ell]|\geq\mathsf{d}_{t}
.

By
Definition

12.14
, we know that for any two distinct
i,j\in[(\ell+1)-\mathsf{z},\ell]
the sets
Z_{i}
and
Z_{j}
are mutually disjoint and in particular, all
Z_{i}
are disjoint from
V(H)
.
Again following
Definition

12.14
, each
Z_{i}
can be divided into two sets
Z_{i}^{\prime}
and
X_{i}
such that
Z_{i}^{\prime}\cap X_{i}=\{z_{i}\}
, we have
|Z_{i}^{\prime}|\leq\mathsf{d}_{t}
, and
X_{i}
is one side of a separation
(X_{i},Y_{i})
as described in
T5
of
Definition

12.14
, with
X_{i}
taking the role of
S
in
T5
i) and
X^{1}_{i},X^{2}_{i}
being the partitioning of
X_{i}\cap Y_{i}
into the two sets of size
\mathsf{d}_{t}+1
.

We first prove the following claim that shows that these
X_{i}
still connect different districts, with many disjoint paths emanating from
X^{1}_{i}
and
X^{2}_{i}
into disjoint collections of districts.
Let
(G_{1},\Omega_{1}),\ldots,(G_{\ell+1},\Omega_{\ell+1})
be the districts of
\mathfrak{M}
and let
G^{\prime}:=\bigcup_{j\in[\ell+1]}G_{j}-\bigcup_{j\in[\ell+1]}A_{j}
.
We note that, due to our choice of
\mathsf{z}
, we have
G^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j\in[\ell+1]}G_{j}-\bigcup_{j\in[\ell-\mathsf{z}]}A_{j}
.

Claim 14.14.1
.

For each
i\in[(\ell+1)-\mathsf{z},\ell]
with
Z_{i}\neq\emptyset
, there exist two disjoint, non-empty sets of integers
I_{1}^{i},I_{2}^{i}\subseteq[\ell+1]
such that for both
j\in[2]
, there exists an
X^{j}_{i}
-
(\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in I_{j}^{i}}V(\Omega_{j^{\prime}}))
-linkage of order
\mathsf{d}_{t}+1
in
G^{\prime}-\bigcup_{i^{\prime}\in[(\ell+1)-\mathsf{z},i-1]}Z_{i^{\prime}}
.

Proof of
Claim

14.14.1
:

Let
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P}\setminus\{\mathcal{P}_{j}\colon j\in[i+1,\ell]\})
be the
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,i)
-transaction mesh derived from
\mathfrak{M}
with the districts
(G_{1}^{i},\Omega_{1}^{i}),\ldots,(G_{i+1}^{i},\Omega_{i+1}^{i})
.
Furthermore, let
(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P}\setminus\{\mathcal{P}_{j}\colon j\in[i,\ell]\})
be the
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,i-1)
-transaction mesh derived from
\mathfrak{M}
with the districts
(G_{1}^{i-1},\Omega_{1}^{i-1}),\ldots,(G_{i}^{i-1},\Omega_{i}^{i-1})
and let
j_{i}\in[i]
be the integer such that
\mathcal{P}_{i}
is found in the
C_{s-s^{\prime}i}^{j_{1},i-1}
-society of
(G_{j_{i}}^{i-1},\Omega_{j_{i}}^{i-1})
, where
\{C_{1}^{j_{i},i-1},\ldots,C_{s-(s^{\prime}(i-1)-1)}^{j_{i},i-1}\}
is the district nest of
(G_{j_{i}}^{i-1},\Omega_{j_{i}}^{i-1})
.

By our choice of
Z_{i}
, according to
T5
i) of
Definition

12.14
, there exists an
X^{1}_{i}
-
\bigcup_{h=1}^{i+1}V(\Omega_{h}^{i})
-linkage
\mathcal{L}^{1}
of order
\mathsf{d}_{t}+1
in
G^{\prime\prime}=\bigcup_{j\in[i+1]}G_{j}^{i}-(\bigcup_{j\in[\ell-\mathsf{z}]}A_{j}\cup\bigcup_{j\in[(\ell+1)-\mathsf{z},i]}Z_{i})
and an
X^{2}_{i}
-
\bigcup_{h=1}^{i+1}V(\Omega_{h}^{i})
-linkage
\mathcal{L}^{2}
of order
\mathsf{d}_{t}+1
in
G^{\prime\prime}
, such that
\mathcal{L}^{1}\cup\mathcal{L}^{2}
is itself a linkage that is disjoint from
X_{i}
, except for one endpoint of each path.
In fact, the existence of the separation in
T5
i) implies that we can partition
[i+1]
into two sets
W_{j_{i}}
and
W_{i+1}
, such that
h\in W_{h}
for both
h\in\{j_{i},i+1\}
and
\mathcal{L}^{1}
is an
X_{1}^{i}
-
\bigcup_{h\in W_{j_{i}}}V(\Omega_{h}^{i})
-linkage and
\mathcal{L}^{2}
is an
X_{2}^{i}
-
\bigcup_{h\in W_{i+1}}V(\Omega_{h}^{i})
-linkage.
Note that
X_{i}
is entirely disjoint from
H
as well.

Thus to find the desired paths, we can follow each of the paths in
\mathcal{L}^{1}\cup\mathcal{L}^{2}
starting from
X_{i}
until they meet a vertex of
\bigcup_{j^{\prime}\in[\ell+1]}V(\Omega_{j^{\prime}})
.
This is guaranteed to happen, since these paths must intersect
H
, as we have
\bigcup_{h=1}^{i+1}V(\Omega_{h}^{i})\subseteq V(H)
and
H
has a vortex-free rendition.
We collect the resulting paths originating from
\mathcal{L}^{1}
in
\mathcal{R}^{1}
and those originating from
\mathcal{L}^{2}
in
\mathcal{R}^{2}
and let
I_{j}^{i}\coloneqq\{j\in[\ell+1]\colon R\in\mathcal{R}^{i}\text{ and }R\text{ has an endpoint in }X_{j}.\}
for both
j\in[2]
.

Suppose there exists some
j\in I_{1}^{i}\cap I_{2}^{i}
.
Then let
R\in\mathcal{R}^{1}\cup\mathcal{R}^{2}
be one of the paths that has an endpoint
v
in
V(\Omega_{j})
.
In particular, we must have
v\in V(G_{j}\cap H)
and thus
R
must intersect all paths of the district-nest of
(G_{j},\Omega_{j})
.
Thus there exists an
X_{1}^{i}
-
X_{2}^{i}
-path in
G^{\prime\prime}
that avoids
Z_{i}\setminus(X_{1}^{i}\cup X_{2}^{i})
, contradicting the fact that
V(\Omega_{j_{i}}^{i})
and
V(\Omega_{i+1}^{i})
lie in different connected components of
G^{\prime\prime}
.

\blacksquare

This pair of sets
I_{1}^{i},I_{2}^{i}
describes districts which were all interconnected in
G^{\prime}-\bigcup_{i^{\prime}\in[(\ell+1)-\mathsf{z},i-1]}Z_{i^{\prime}}
, but after the deletion of
Z_{i}
the districts corresponding to
I_{1}^{i}
and those corresponding to
I_{2}^{i}
have been disconnected from each other.

We now apply
Theorem

13.1
to
G^{\prime}
.
If this yields a
K_{t}
-minor, we are done.
Thus we may suppose that this instead results in us finding sets
S\subseteq V(G^{\prime})
and
J\subseteq[\ell+1]
with
|S|\leq 8t^{3}-16
and
|J|\leq 64t^{3}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)-128
such that each
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in[\ell+1]\}
-path in
G^{\prime}-S
is a
\{V(\Omega_{i})~\!\colon\!~i\in J\}
-path and each district
(G_{j},\Omega_{j})
with
j\in[\ell+1]\setminus J
has a vortex-free rendition in a disk.
Note that by definition
|S|+|J|\leq\mathsf{d}_{t}\leq|I|
.

We observe that according to
T5
i) of
Definition

12.14
,
Z_{i}
and
Z_{j}
are disjoint for any two distinct
i,j\in I
.
Thus, if we let
I^{\prime}\subseteq I
be the set of integers
i\in I
such that
Z_{i}\cap S=\emptyset
, we have
|J|\leq|I^{\prime}|
.

With the help of
Claim

14.14.1
and the fact that
X_{i}
induces a connected subgraph of
G
, we observe that for all
i\in I^{\prime}
there exists a
\{V(\Omega_{j})~\!\colon\!~j\in I_{1}^{i}\}
-
\{V(\Omega_{j})~\!\colon\!~j\in I_{2}^{i}\}
-path in
G^{\prime}-S
, since
|S|<\mathsf{d}_{t}+1
.
Thus for each
i\in I^{\prime}
, the set
J
must include two distinct integers
i_{1}^{i}\in I_{1}^{i}
and
i_{2}^{i}\in I_{2}^{i}
.

Let
I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{z}\}
such that for any two
h,j\in[z]
we have
i_{h}<i_{j}
if and only if
h<j
.
We inductively define a forest
T_{i}
, for
i\in[0,z]
, that describes the structure of the sets in
\mathcal{F}
.
For this purpose, let
\mathcal{F}^{i}
be a partitioning of
[\ell+1]
into as few sets as possible such that for each
F\in\mathcal{F}^{i}
with
|F|\geq 2
there exists a
V(\Omega_{i})
-
V(\Omega_{j})
-path in
G^{\prime}_{i}\coloneqq G^{\prime}-\bigcup_{i^{\prime}\in[(\ell+1)-\mathsf{z},i-1]}Z_{i^{\prime}}
for all distinct
i,j\in F
.
Thus we have
G^{\prime}_{0}=G^{\prime}
.

We initialise our construction by letting
T_{0}
be the graph in which the sets in
\mathcal{F}^{0}
correspond to the vertices
V(T_{0})=\{v^{0}_{F}~\!\colon\!~F\in\mathcal{F}^{0}\}
and there are no edges in
T_{0}
.
For each
i\in[z]
, we let
T_{i}
be defined by adding the vertices
\{v^{i}_{F}~\!\colon\!~F\in\mathcal{F}^{i}\}
to
T_{i-1}
and adding an edge between
v^{i}_{F}
and
v^{i-1}_{F^{\prime}}
if
F\subseteq F^{\prime}
.
We claim that this results in each vertex that is added to construct
T_{i}
having degree 1.
This is a simple consequence of the fact that
G^{\prime}_{i}=G^{\prime}_{i}-Z_{i_{i-1}}
, as both
\mathcal{F}^{i}
and
\mathcal{F}^{i-1}
reflect the structure of the connected components containing the districts of these graphs.
In particular,
\mathcal{F}^{i}
is a refinement of
\mathcal{F}^{i-1}
.
We let
W\coloneq T_{z}
and we further consider all trees in
W
to be rooted at their unique vertex in
\{v^{0}_{F}~\!\colon\!~F\in\mathcal{F}^{0}\}
.

For each
i_{j}\in I
, let
F_{j}\in\mathcal{F}^{j-1}
be the set with
I_{1}^{j}\cup I_{2}^{j}\subseteq F_{j}
, which must exist since
Z_{j}
is non-empty.
Note that
v^{j-1}_{F_{j}}
has at least two children as
Z_{j}
separates two sets
V(\Omega_{i})
and
V(\Omega_{h})
with
i\in I_{2}^{j}
and
h\in I_{2}^{j}
, according to
Claim

14.14.1
.
Using this fact, we let
W_{1}^{j},W_{2}^{j}
be two components of
W-v^{j-1}_{F_{j}}
, for each
i_{j}\in I
, such that for both
h\in[2]
, the tree
W_{h}^{j}
contains a unique child of
v^{j}_{F_{j}}
and
W_{h}^{j}
contains a leaf
v^{z}_{F}
with
F\cap I_{h}^{j}\neq\emptyset
.
We call this leaf
v^{j}_{h}
.
Thanks to the choices we made during our constructions, we can now verify the following claim, which leads us to the end of our proof.

Claim 14.14.2
.

The set
\{v^{j}_{1},v^{j}_{2}~\!\colon\!~j\in[z]\}
contains at least
z+1
vertices.

Proof of
Claim

14.14.2
:

We will prove this by proving a stronger statement by induction on
z
.
In particular, we want to show that the set
\mathcal{S}_{h}\coloneqq\{W^{j}_{1},W^{j}_{2}~\!\colon\!~j\in[h]\}
contains
h+1
pairwise disjoint trees for all
h\in[z]
.
The case
h=1
is immediately settled by
Claim

14.14.1
.
Suppose therefore that
h>1
and
\mathcal{S}_{h-1}\subseteq\mathcal{S}_{h}
contains
h
pairwise disjoint trees
W_{1},\ldots,W_{h}
.
If either
W^{h}_{1}
or
W^{h}_{2}
are disjoint from the trees
W_{1},\ldots,W_{h}
, we are done.

Suppose w.l.o.g. that
W_{1}
intersects
W^{h}_{1}\cup W^{h}_{2}
.
As the root of
W_{1}
has depth at most
h-1
, this must mean that
W_{1}
contains the common parent of the roots of
W^{h}_{1}
and
W^{h}_{2}
.
Thus, by definition of the elements of
\mathcal{S}_{h}
, we must have
W^{h}_{1}\cup W^{h}_{2}\subseteq W_{1}
and
W^{h}_{1}\cup W^{h}_{2}
is therefore disjoint from the trees
W_{2},\ldots,W_{h}
.
We conclude that
W^{h}_{1},W^{h}_{2},W_{2},\ldots,W_{h}
are
h+1
pairwise disjoint trees in
\mathcal{S}_{h}
.

\blacksquare

However, as
z\geq\mathsf{d}_{t}
, this contradicts the properties of the set
J
we got from our application of
Theorem

13.1
.
Thus we will find a
K_{t}
-minor with the desired properties.
∎

Our last lemma will actually not require us to use
Theorem

13.1
.
The argument we present here is fairly technical, since we will have to delve into the internals of
Definition

12.1
.
But the intuition of our approach is simple.
Similar to
Lemma

13.2
, we will find an extended surface-wall that captures each of the areas inside of the transactions that host crosses which intersect the apices of
\mathfrak{M}
(see
M5
of
Definition

12.1
) and since
a^{\prime}\geq k
, this lets us find a
K_{t}
-minor with the desired properties thanks to
Lemma

5.2
.

Lemma 14.15
.

Let
t,s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell
be non-negative integers with
k\coloneqq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)
,
s^{\prime}\geq 5t+74
,
s\geq s^{\prime}\ell+(8t+96)k+2t+25
, and
p\geq 2s
.
Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with an
(s,s^{\prime},a,p,\ell)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}=(H,\Omega,\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{P})
and let
(a^{\prime},z,f,h)
be the
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signature of
\mathfrak{M}
.

If
a^{\prime}\geq k
the graph
G
contains a
K_{t}
-minor model controlled by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct cycles from
\mathcal{C}
.
Furthermore, there exists an algorithm that finds this
K_{t}
-minor model in time
\mathbf{O}(t^{3}|E(G)|)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{T}=(T,\mathfrak{M},\phi,\mathcal{A}=\{A_{1},\ldots,A_{\ell}\},\mathcal{Z},\mathcal{F},\mathfrak{L})
be the
\mathfrak{M}
-tree of precision
\mathsf{d}_{t}
that witnesses the signature of
\mathfrak{M}
and let
\mathfrak{P}=\{\mathcal{P}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{P}_{\ell}\}
.
We let
(H_{j}^{i},\Omega_{j}^{i})
for all
i\in[0,\ell]
and
j\in[i+1]
be defined as in
Definition

12.1
, with
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{j}=\{C_{1}^{i,j},\ldots,C_{s-(s^{\prime}i+1)}^{i,j}\}
being the corresponding nests.
Let
k\coloneqq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)
.

Let
i\in[\ell]
be chosen such that
A_{i}\neq\emptyset
.
As in
Definition

12.1
, let
j_{i}\in[i]
be the integer such that
\mathcal{P}_{i}=\{P_{1}^{i},\ldots,P_{p}^{i}\}
is a planar transaction that is naturally indexed in
(H_{i}\cup\bigcup\mathcal{P},\Omega_{i})
, where
(H_{i},\Omega_{i})
is the
C_{s-s^{\prime}i}^{j_{i},i-1}
-society in the cylindrical rendition of
(H_{j_{i}}^{i-1},\Omega_{j_{i}}^{i-1})
.
Let
G^{\prime\prime}
be the component of
G-(\bigcup_{j=1}^{i}V(\Omega_{j}^{i-1})\cup\bigcup_{j=1}^{i}A_{j}\cup V(\Omega^{*}_{i}))
that contains
C_{1}^{j_{i},i-1}
, let
G^{\prime}\coloneqq G[G^{\prime\prime}\cup\bigcup_{j=1}^{i}V(\Omega_{j}^{i-1})\cup V(\Omega^{*}_{i})]
, let
Q^{i}_{L}\coloneqq P^{i}_{\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor}
, and
Q^{i}_{R}\coloneqq P^{i}_{\lceil\nicefrac{{(p+1)}}{{2}}\rceil}
.
Further, let
(U_{i},\Psi_{i})
be the
\mathcal{P}_{i}
-strip society in
(G^{\prime},\Omega_{i})
and let
(U_{i}^{\prime},\Psi_{i}^{\prime})
be the
\{Q^{i}_{L},Q^{i}_{R}\}
-society in
(U_{i},\Psi_{i})
.
According to the point
M3
in
Definition

12.1
,
U_{i}
is disjoint from
\bigcup_{j\in[i]\setminus\{j_{i}\}}\mathcal{C}_{j}^{i}
, and since
A_{i}\neq\emptyset
, the society
(G_{i}[V(U_{i}^{\prime})\cup A_{i}],\Psi_{i}^{\prime})
has a cross.
We let
G_{i}\coloneqq G^{\prime}[V(U_{i}^{\prime})\cup A_{i}]
.

Recall that
(H,\Omega)
has a vortex-free rendition
\rho_{H}
in a disk
\Delta
according to
Definition

12.1
.
We are interested in two more societies in relation to the
i\in[\ell]
we have chosen above.
First, consider the paths
S^{i}_{L}\coloneqq P^{i}_{\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-(s^{\prime}-1)}
and
S^{i}_{R}\coloneqq P^{i}_{\lceil\nicefrac{{(p+1)}}{{2}}\rceil+s^{\prime}-1}
.
Recall that
V(\Omega_{i})\subseteq V(C_{s-s^{\prime}i}^{j_{i},i-1})
and note that
\{S^{i}_{L},S^{i}_{R}\}
is a transaction in
(H_{i}\cup S^{i}_{L}\cup S^{i}_{R},\Omega_{i})

Thus
S^{i}_{L}\cup S^{i}_{R}\cup C_{s-s^{\prime}i}^{j_{i},i-1}
contain a cycle
C^{\prime}_{i}
such that
S^{i}_{L}\cup S^{i}_{R}\subseteq C^{\prime}_{i}
and the trace of
C^{\prime}_{i}
in
\rho_{H}
separates the traces of
Q^{i}_{L}
and
Q^{i}_{R}
from the boundary of
\Delta
.
As a consequence, the disk
\Delta_{i}
within
\Delta
defined by the trace of
C^{\prime}_{i}
that contains the traces of
Q^{i}_{L}
and
Q^{i}_{R}
defines the
\Delta_{i}
-society
(H_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
.

We observe that
V(\Psi_{i}^{\prime})\subseteq V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
and thus
(H_{i}^{\prime}\cup G_{i},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
contains a cross.
Let
T_{L}^{i},T_{R}^{i}
be the traces of
Q^{i}_{L},Q^{i}_{R}
in
\rho_{H}
, let
e\in E(Q^{i}_{L})\setminus E(H_{i})
be an edge of
Q^{i}_{L}
found outside of
E(H_{i})
– which must exist since
Q^{i}_{L}
comes from an exposed transaction – and let
e_{T}
be the curve in
T_{L}^{i}
representing
e
.
We use
e_{T}
to define the inclusion-wise minimal subdisk
\Delta_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq\Delta_{i}
defined by the trace in
\rho_{H}
of a cycle in
S^{i}_{L}\cup S^{i}_{R}\cup C_{2t+25}^{j,i-1}\subseteq H
that contains
e_{T}
.
The cycle whose trace in
\rho_{H}
bounds
\Delta_{i}^{\prime}
must contain edges of both
S^{i}_{L}
and
S^{i}_{R}
.
We let
(H_{i}^{\prime\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime\prime})
be the
\Delta_{i}^{\prime}
-society in
\rho_{H}
and let
G_{i}^{\prime}\coloneqq H_{i}^{\prime\prime}\cup G_{i}
.

Consider
M5
of
Definition

12.1
and in particular, the fact that the vertices of
A_{i}
have no neighbours in
V(H_{i})
.
We conclude that, since
(H_{i}^{\prime}\cup G_{i},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
contains a cross,
(G_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
contains a cross.

Note that
H_{i}^{\prime\prime}
contains subpaths of
P_{j}^{i}
for each
j\in[\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-(s^{\prime}-2),\lceil\nicefrac{{(p+1)}}{{2}}\rceil+s^{\prime}-2]
and thus
H_{i}^{\prime\prime}
contains subpaths of
2s^{\prime}-2=8t+48
paths from
\mathcal{P}_{i}
.
Furthermore,
H_{i}^{\prime\prime}
contains subpaths of the cycles
C_{1}^{j,i-1},\ldots,C_{2t+24}^{j,i-1}
.
For all
j\in[2t+24]
, this allows us to define a cycle
C_{j}^{i}
via the boundary of the inclusion-wise minimal subdisk of
\Delta_{i}
that contains
e_{T}
such that

C_{j}^{i\star}\subseteq C^{j,i-1}_{j}\cup P^{i}_{\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-(4t+48+j)}\cup P^{i}_{\lceil\nicefrac{{(p+1)}}{{2}}\rceil+4t+48+j}.

We note that the cycles
C_{1}^{i\star},\ldots,C_{2t+24}^{i\star}
are pairwise disjoint.
Since
G_{i}^{\prime}
is derived from the
\{Q^{i}_{L},Q^{i}_{R}\}
-society in
(U_{i},\Psi_{i})
and the vertices
A_{i}
have no neighbours in
V(H_{i})
, the society
(G_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
has a cylindrical rendition in
\Delta_{i}^{\prime}
in which
\{C_{1}^{i\star},\ldots,C_{2t+24}^{i\star}\}
is a nest of order
2t+24
.
This allows us to use
Lemma

8.4
to find a cozy nest
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime}=\{C_{1}^{i},\ldots,C_{2t+24}^{i}\}
of order
2t+24
in
(G_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
.
Furthermore, using the paths
P_{j}^{i}
with
j\in[\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-(4t+48),\lceil\nicefrac{{(p+1)}}{{2}}\rceil+4t+48]
, we can find a radial linkage
\mathcal{R}_{i}
for
\mathcal{C}_{i}^{\prime}
of order
8t+96
in
G_{i}^{\prime}
.

Let
I\coloneq\{i\in[\ell]~\!\colon\!~A_{i}\neq\emptyset\}
and let
I^{\prime}\subseteq I
be a subset of
I
of size exactly
k
, which exists thanks to
a^{\prime}\geq k
.
We have thus, for every
i\in I
, constructed a society
(G_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
with a cylindrical rendition in a disk
\Delta_{i}^{\prime}
in which a nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}
of order
10t+121=5(2t+24)+1
and a radial linkage
\mathcal{R}_{i}
for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}
of order
8t+96=4(2t+24)
exist.
Furthermore,
(G_{i}^{\prime},\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
contains a cross.
By construction, for any two distinct
i,j\in I
, the graphs
G_{i}^{\prime}
and
G_{j}^{\prime}
are disjoint.
We let
X_{i}
be the
8t+96
endpoints of
\mathcal{R}_{i}
in
V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime})
, where we note that we can choose
\mathcal{R}_{i}
such that
X_{i}
are found in a segment of
\Omega_{i}^{\prime}
that is disjoint from any path
P_{j}^{i}\in\mathcal{P}_{i}
with
j\not\in[\lfloor\nicefrac{{p}}{{2}}\rfloor-(4t+48),\lceil\nicefrac{{(p+1)}}{{2}}\rceil+4t+48]
.
We set
X\coloneqq\bigcup_{i\in I^{\prime}}X_{i}
.

Let
\mathcal{P}\in\mathfrak{P}
be a transaction of
\mathfrak{M}
such that there exists a subpath
Y^{\prime}
of
C_{s-s^{\prime}}
that contains both endpoints of every member of
\mathcal{P}
.
Such a transaction must exist, since
\ell\geq 1
, according to
Definition

12.1
.
Let
Y
be the set of all vertices that are the first vertex encountered on
C_{s-s^{\prime}}
when traversing along some member of
\mathcal{P}
starting from
Y^{\prime}
in either direction of traversal.

Using
Menger’s Theorem
, we conclude that there exists a linkage
\mathcal{L}
of order
(8t+96)k
between
X
and
Y
, as there are at least that many cycles of
\mathcal{C}
that have an index lower than
s-s^{\prime}
, as well as at least that many cycles of each
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}
, paths from
\mathcal{P}
, and paths in each
\mathcal{R}_{i}
, each themselves contained in
\mathcal{P}_{i}\in\mathfrak{P}
.
At least one of these families would need to be fully separated from the others by a separator of size at most
(8t+96)k-1
, which is impossible.
Moreover,
\mathcal{L}
is contained in the inner graph of
C_{s-s^{\prime}}
and disjoint from
G_{i}^{\prime\prime}-V(\Omega_{i}^{\prime\prime})
.

Next, we need to turn
\mathcal{L}
into a linkage that is orthogonal to the
2t+24
innermost cycles of each nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}
, as well as the cycles
C_{s-(s^{\prime}+2t+23)},\ldots,C_{s-s^{\prime}}
.
For each
i\in I
, we apply
Lemma

10.2
to
\mathcal{R}_{i}
and
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}
to get an orthogonal, radial linkage
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime}
for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}_{i}
that is end-identical to
\mathcal{R}_{i}
.
Let
X_{i}^{\star}
be the set of first vertices encounter on the cycle
C_{2t+25}^{i}
when following along the paths in
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime}
starting from
C_{1}^{i}
and let
X^{\star}=\bigcup_{i\in I}X_{i}^{\star}
.
Then, we follow along
\mathcal{L}
, starting from its endpoints in
C_{s-s^{\prime}}
until we meet the cycle
C_{s-(s^{\prime}+2t+23+(8t+96)k)}
.
Let
\mathcal{J}
be the resulting
V(C_{s-s^{\prime}})
-
V(C_{s-(s^{\prime}+2t+23+(8t+96)k))}
-linkage between.
Another application of
Lemma

10.2
yields a linkage
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
of the same size which is orthogonal to
\{C_{s-(s^{\prime}+2t+23+(8t+96)k)},\ldots,C_{s-s^{\prime}}\}
and end-identical to
\mathcal{J}
.
Finally, let
Y^{\star}
be the set of all vertices on
C_{s-(s^{\prime}+2t+23)}
we encounter when traversing along the paths of
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
starting from
C_{s-s^{\prime}}
.

Consider the graph
H^{\star}
obtained by taking the intersection of the inner graph of
C_{s-(s^{\prime}+2t+23)}
and the outer graphs of the cycles
C^{i}_{2t+25}
for all
i\in I
– taken in the vortex-free rendition of
(H,\Omega)
in a disk that exists according to
Definition

12.1
– by adding the cycles
C_{s-(s^{\prime}+2t+23)}
and
C^{i}_{2t+25}
back in for all
i\in I
.
The graph
H^{\star}
still contains the cycles
C_{s-(s^{\prime}+2t+23+(8t+96)k)},\ldots,C_{s-s^{\prime}}
as well as, for each
i\in I
, the cycles
C^{i}_{2t+25},\ldots,C^{i}_{10t+121}
.
An application of
Proposition

2.1
yields an
X^{\star}
-
Y^{\star}
-linkage
\mathcal{J}^{\star}
of order
(8t+96)k
in
H^{\star}
.

We can now combine
\mathcal{J}^{\star}
with the orthogonal linkages
\mathcal{R}_{i}^{\prime}
for all
i\in I
and the orthogonal linkage
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
to obtain a
V(C_{s-s^{\prime}})
-
V(\bigcup_{i\in I}C_{1}^{i})
-linkage
\mathcal{L}^{\star}
whose intersection with any of the cycles
C_{1}^{i},\ldots,C_{2t+24}^{i}
, for all
i\in I
, and the cycles
C_{s-(s^{\prime}+2t+23)}
is a single, non-empty path.
Moreover
\mathcal{L}^{\star}
has
8t+96
endpoints on each
C_{1}^{i}
for each
i\in I
.

As a consequence
\{C_{s-s^{\prime}},\ldots,C_{s-(s^{\prime}+2t+23)}\}
together with
\mathcal{L}^{\star}
and the cycles
\{C^{i}_{1},\ldots,C^{i}_{2t+24}\}
for each
i\in I
form an extended
(2t+24)
-surface-wall.
This then yields the desired
K_{t}
-minor via
Corollary

5.3
.
∎

14.4
Classifying societies

Now that we have gathered numerous tools, we are finally ready to stick them all together to prove the main theorem of this section.
This mainly consists of us applying
Lemma

14.9
until we either run into the conditions in which
Lemma

14.7
tells us that we are done, or until we have accumulated enough iterations that we can use the results from
Section

14.3
to show that we can find a
K_{t}
-minor model with the properties we desire.

Proof of
Theorem

14.1
.

We give the following estimates for the functions in the statement, including some additional functions useful in the proof.
Recall the definition of
\mathsf{d}_{t}
from
Definition

14.10
and note that
\mathsf{d}_{t}\in\mathbf{O}(t^{3})
.

\displaystyle\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ (t-3)(t-4)\mathsf{d}_{t}^{2}\in\mathbf{O}(t^{8})

\displaystyle\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ 8t^{3}+3\mathsf{d}_{t}^{2}+2\mathsf{d}_{t}\in\mathbf{O}(t^{6})

\displaystyle\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ 8t^{3}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)\in\mathbf{O}(t^{8})

\displaystyle\mathsf{loss}^{\mathsf{iter}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,\mathsf{d}_{t})\in\mathbf{O}(t^{3})

\displaystyle\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{loss}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}\in\mathbf{O}(t^{3})

\displaystyle\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \max(k,5t+74)\in\mathbf{O}(t+k)

\displaystyle\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ (8t+96)(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4))+(2t+24)(8t^{3}+20)\ +

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k)\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)+\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t)\ +

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{nest}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,\mathsf{d}_{t},\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t),\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k),\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t),\mathsf{d}_{t})\in\mathbf{O}(t^{9}+t^{8}k)

\displaystyle\mathsf{link}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k,p)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ (64t^{3}-128)(2t+24)+32\mathsf{d}_{t}+8\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)+

\displaystyle\ 4\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k)(p-1)+\mathsf{link}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t,\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k),p)\in\mathbf{O}(t^{14}+t^{9}p+t^{8}kp)

\displaystyle\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k,p)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{apex}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t,\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k),\mathsf{link}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k,p),p)

\displaystyle\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k,p)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\ \mathsf{depth}_{\ref{lem:buildtransactionmesh}}(t,\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k),p,\mathsf{link}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k,p),\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t),\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t),\mathsf{d}_{t},\mathsf{d}_{t})

We note that
\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k,p)\in\mathbf{O}(t^{35}p^{2}+t^{30}p^{3}+t^{29}kp^{3}+t^{34}kp^{2}+t^{28}k^{2}p^{3})
and furthermore,
\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k,p)\in\mathbf{O}(t^{50}p+t^{45}p^{2}+t^{44}kp^{2}+t^{49}kp+t^{44}kp^{2}+t^{43}k^{2}p^{2})
.
These estimates are a little cumbersome to state but do help us with slightly optimising our analysis for the bounds of the local structure theorem later on.

Let
p^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathsf{link}_{\ref{lem:everydistrictboundeddepth}}(t,k,p)
, let
s^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k)
, let
a\coloneqq\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)
, and let
a^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathsf{loss}^{\mathsf{iter}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)
.

We start by recalling the observation from
Definition

12.1
according to which any society
(G,\Omega)
with a cylindrical rendition and a nest of order
s
defines an
(s,s^{\prime},a,p^{\prime},0)
-transaction mesh.
For our
(G,\Omega)
, we let this transaction mesh be
\mathfrak{M}_{0}
.
We will now iteratively apply
Lemma

14.9
until we find one of our desired outcomes.
The main task of our proof now is to use the tools we proved in
Section

14.3
to show that this happens after at most
\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)
iterations.

Let
i\in[0,\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}-1]
and suppose we have already built a sequence of padded transaction meshes
\mathfrak{M}_{0},\mathfrak{M}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{M}_{j}
such that for all
j\in[0,i]
,
\mathfrak{M}_{j}
is an
(s-j(a^{\prime}+s^{\prime}),s^{\prime},a,p^{\prime}-2a^{\prime}j,j)
-transaction mesh with the
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signature
\bar{x}_{j}
.
In particular, we suppose that we also have a sequence of
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signatures
\bar{x}_{0},\bar{x}_{1},\ldots,\bar{x}_{j}
, such that
\bar{x}_{h-1}\leq\bar{x}_{h}
for all
h\in[i]
.
For each
j\in[0,i]
, we let
\bar{x}_{j}=(a^{\prime\prime}_{j},z_{j},f_{j},h_{j})
.

If
a^{\prime\prime}_{i}\geq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t_{4})
, then according to
Lemma

14.15
we can find a
K_{t}
-minor model fitting the conditions we laid out in our statement.
Thus we have
a^{\prime\prime}_{j}<\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t_{4})
for all
j\in[i]
.
If there exists an interval
[j,l]\subseteq[i]
such that
a^{\prime\prime}_{h}=a^{\prime\prime}_{h^{\prime}}
for all
h,h^{\prime}\in[j,l]
and
z_{l}\geq\mathsf{d}_{t}
, then, according to
Lemma

14.14
, this again yields the
K_{t}
-minor model we desire and thus our proof is complete.

This allows us to conclude two things:
Firstly, that the union of the apices of the transactions in
\mathfrak{P}_{i}
has size at most
a\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t_{4})
.
Let
\mathfrak{T}_{i}=(T_{i},\mathfrak{M}_{i},\phi_{i},\mathcal{A}_{i},\mathcal{Z}_{i}=\{Z_{1}^{i},\ldots,Z_{\ell}^{i}\},\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathfrak{L}_{i})
be the
\mathfrak{M}_{i}
-tree and let
\mathsf{z}\in[i]
be the maximal integer such that
A_{j}=\emptyset
for all
j\in[(i+1)-\mathsf{z},i]
.
Then secondly,
Z_{i}\coloneqq\bigcup_{j=(\ell+1)-\mathsf{z}}^{\ell}B_{\mathfrak{T}}(Z_{j}^{i})
has order at most
3(\mathsf{d}_{t}^{2})+2\mathsf{d}_{t}
.

We now let
\{(G_{j},\Omega_{j})\}_{j\in[i+1]}
be the districts of
\mathfrak{M}_{i}
each with a cylindrical rendition
\rho_{j}
, and let
\mathcal{C}_{j}=\{C_{1}^{j},\ldots,C_{s-(is^{\prime}+1)}^{j}\}
be the district-nest of
(G_{j},\Omega_{j})
for all
j\in[i+1]
, with
(G_{j}^{\prime},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
being
C_{s-(i+1)s^{\prime}}^{j}
-society in
\rho_{j}
.

Suppose that for all
j\in[i+1]
the society
(G_{j}^{\prime},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
has depth less than
\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k,p)
.
We first discuss the case in which
i=0
.
In this case, we can simply ask for a
V(\Omega)
-
\sigma(c_{0})
-linkage of order
4k
in
G
via
Proposition

2.1
.
Should this not exist, we receive a separator
S
with
|S|<4k
, which allows us to find a vortex-free rendition of
(G,\Omega)
derived from
\rho
by chucking the remains of
\sigma(c_{0})
into some arbitrary cell.
This clearly satisfies the fourth option in our statement.
Thus we may assume that there exist a radial linkage of order
4k
for
\mathcal{C}
in
G
, which we can orthogonalise via
Lemma

10.2
, again allowing us to satisfy the fourth option in our statement.
Should
i\neq 0
, we can accomplish the same thing by applying
Lemma

14.7
.
This yields one of the four options that we desire in our statement, thanks to definition of
\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}
and our choice of
p
.

Hence we can assume that
(G_{j}^{\prime},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
has depth at least
\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k,p)
for some
j\in[i+1]
, which allows us to apply
Lemma

14.9
, again due to our definition for
\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}
, as well as the constants
p
and
a
.
This either yields a
K_{t}
-minor that satisfies the first point of our theorem, a crosscap transaction of order
p
that satisfies the second point, or we can find a transaction
\mathcal{P}_{i+1}
in
(G_{j}^{\prime},\Omega_{j}^{\prime})
with a set of co-conspirators which builds a padded
(s-(i+1)(a^{\prime}+s^{\prime}),s^{\prime},a,p^{\prime}-2a^{\prime}(i+1),i+1)
-transaction mesh
\mathfrak{M}_{i+1}
in
(G,\Omega)
with the
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signature
\bar{x}_{i+1}
.

As a consequence, we are either done after at most
\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)-1
iterations, or we can find transaction meshes
\mathfrak{M}_{0},\mathfrak{M}_{1},\ldots,\mathfrak{M}_{\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)}
with
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signatures defined as above.
Recall that we have already determined that
a^{\prime\prime}_{j}<\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t_{4})
and additionally know that there does not exist an interval
[j,l]\subseteq[\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)]
such that
a^{\prime\prime}_{h}=a^{\prime\prime}_{h^{\prime}}
for all
h,h^{\prime}\in[j,l]
and
z_{l}\geq\mathsf{d}_{t}
.

Suppose there exists an interval
[j,l]\subseteq[\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)]
with
|[j,l]|\geq 2\mathsf{d}_{t}
such that
a^{\prime\prime}_{i}=a^{\prime\prime}_{i^{\prime}}
and
z_{i}=z_{i^{\prime}}
for all
i,i^{\prime}\in[j,l]
.
As a consequence, according to the definition of
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signatures in
Definition

12.14
, we must have
h_{i}\leq h_{i+1}
for all
i\in[j,l-1]
.
If
h_{l}>\mathsf{d}_{t}
, then
Corollary

14.13
tells us that we can find a
K_{t}
-minor with the desired properties.
Thus, we can also assume that
h_{i}\leq\mathsf{d}_{t}
for all
i\in[j,l]
.
We claim that under this restriction,
\mathfrak{M}_{l}
allows us to find a
K_{t}
-minor model with the desired properties.

Claim 14.15.1
.

For each
i\in[j,l-1]
, if
h_{i}=h_{i+1}
then
f_{i}<f_{i+1}
.

Proof of
Claim

14.15.1
:

According to the definition of
\mathsf{d}_{t}
-signatures in
Definition

12.14
, for each
i\in[j+1,l]
for which we have
h_{i-1}=h_{i}
, the two new districts in
\mathfrak{M}_{i}
constructed via
\mathcal{P}_{i}
were separated without the deletion of any vertices.
In particular, no new vertices are deleted starting from the construction of
\mathfrak{M}_{j}
all the way until we have constructed
\mathfrak{M}_{l}
.
Recall that
Lemma

14.9
provides us with a set of co-conspirators
\mathcal{W}_{i}
for each
\mathcal{P}_{i}
.

Now for each
i\in[j,l]
, we let
(G_{j_{i}},\Omega_{j_{i}})
be the district of
\mathfrak{M}_{i-1}
with the cylindrical rendition
\rho_{i}
such that
\mathcal{P}_{i}
is a transaction in the
C^{i}_{s-(i+1)s^{\prime})}
-society
(G_{j_{i}}^{\prime},\Omega_{j_{i}}^{\prime})
of
\rho_{i}
.
Further, let
(G_{1}^{i},\Omega_{1}^{i}),(G_{2}^{i},\Omega_{2}^{i})
be the two unique, distinct districts of
\mathfrak{M}_{i}
such that
V(\Omega_{i^{\prime}}^{i})\cap V(\Omega_{j_{i}}^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
for both
i^{\prime}\in[2]
.
Note that therefore, among the co-conspirators
\mathcal{W}_{i}
there exist both paths with endpoints in
V(\Omega_{i^{\prime}}^{i})
for both
i^{\prime}\in[2]
.
Due to
V(\Omega_{1}^{i})
and
V(\Omega_{2}^{i})
being separated in the union
U_{i}
of the districts of
\mathfrak{M}_{i}
, we know that
\mathcal{W}_{i}
cannot be a snitch.

Let
F\in\mathcal{F}_{i-1}
be the set with
j_{i}\in F
from
\mathfrak{T}_{i-1}
, which we defined earlier.
Due to
V(\Omega_{1}^{i})
and
V(\Omega_{2}^{i})
being separated in
U_{i}
and the fact that we did not remove any vertices inside of the vortices of the districts to construct
\mathfrak{M}_{i}
from
\mathfrak{M}_{i-1}
, we know that
|\mathcal{F}_{i}|=|\mathcal{F}_{i-1}|+1
.
In particular, if we let
F_{1},F_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{i}
be the two distinct sets such that
F_{i^{\prime}}
contains the integer corresponding to the district
(G_{i^{\prime}}^{i},\Omega_{i^{\prime}}^{i})
for both
i^{\prime}\in[2]
, we have
\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\setminus\{F\}=\mathcal{F}_{i}\setminus\{F_{1},F_{2}\}
.

Now, if for both
i^{\prime}\in[2]
we either have that
(G_{i^{\prime}}^{i},\Omega_{i^{\prime}}^{i})
has a cross or
|F_{i^{\prime}}|\geq 2
, our claim is prove.
Thus we may suppose this is not the case.
This in particular means that
\mathcal{W}_{i}
cannot contain any crosses and must thus be a pair of deserters.
However, these deserters now themselves verify that these two districts are connected to other districts, which means that we must have
|F_{i^{\prime}}|\geq 2
for both
i^{\prime}\in[2]
.
Thus our claim holds.

\blacksquare

As a consequence of this claim and the fact that
h_{l}\leq\mathsf{d}_{t}
, we know that
f_{l}>\mathsf{d}_{t}
, since
|[j,l]|\geq 2\mathsf{d}_{t}
.
Thanks to
Lemma

14.11
, we know that this means that we can find a
K_{t}
-minor model as desired.
Thus since
\mathsf{d}_{t}>72t^{3}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)-145=8t^{3}-16+64t^{3}-128+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)-1
, we must instead find the
K_{t}
-minor model.
As a consequence no interval
[j,l]\subseteq[\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)]
with
|[j,l]|\geq 2\mathsf{d}_{t}
such that
a^{\prime\prime}_{i}=a^{\prime\prime}_{i^{\prime}}
and
z_{i}=z_{i^{\prime}}
for all
i,i^{\prime}\in[j,l]
can exist.
However, our choice for
\mathsf{iter}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)
makes it impossible for all of the restrictions we have placed on the signatures of our transactions meshes to hold at the same time.
We must therefore be able to find a
K_{t}
-minor model in the way we desire and thus our proof is complete.
∎

15
The local structure theorem

We finally have everything in place to start discussing the proof for the Local Structure Theorem.
On a high level, our proof will start with a large mesh.
We then call the Flat Wall Theorem, that is
theorem

6.2
.
If this does not yield a clique-minor, we have found a large flat mesh
M
.
A large portion of
M
will now be reserved to make sure our tangle agrees with the – eventually – embedded part of the graph.
The remaining part of
M
is used to define a first society
(G,\Omega)
together with a large nest and a radial linkage such that
(G,\Omega)
has a cylindrical rendition and everything that does not belong to the compass of our flat mesh
M
is confined into this vortex.

From here on the proof follows the route already illustrated in
figure

4
.
We will maintain a
\Sigma
-landscape
\Lambda
with a single vortex where
\Sigma
starts out as the sphere.
We iteratively apply the Society Classification Theorem (that is
theorem

14.1
) to
(G,\Omega)
.
This yields one of four outcomes;

i)

A clique-minor which would allow us to terminate the process,

ii)

a small apex set and a flat crosscap transaction,

iii)

a small apex set and a flat handle-transaction, or

iv)

a small apex set and a dissolution of our unique vortex into a small number of bounded depth vortices, each with a nest and a linkage reaching back into the nest of
(G,\Omega)
.

In cases (ii) and (iii) we will call upon one of two technical lemmas – one for the orientable and one for the non-orientable case – to either attach a handle or a crosscap to
\Sigma
, thereby increasing its Euler-genus, to route a small part of the transaction back onto our original mesh, and to install a new society
(G^{\prime}\Omega^{\prime})
, again with a large nest and a unique vortex.
By doing this we maintain our landscape and are able to iterate until either the Euler-genus of our surface is so large that
corollary

5.8
provides us with the forbidden minor, or
theorem

14.1
returns a rendition of
(G^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})
in the disk with bounded breadth and depth.

Recall the definitions of landscapes and layouts from
section

3.7
.
Our version of the Local Structure Theorem reads as follows.

Theorem 15.1
.

There exist functions
\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}},\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{2}\to\mathbb{N}
and
\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{3}\to\mathbb{N}
such that for all non-negative integers
k
,
t\geq 5
, and
r
, every graph
H
on
t
vertices, every graph
G
and every
\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}}(t,r,k)
-mesh
M\subseteq G
one of the following holds.

i)

G
has an
H
-minor controlled by
M
, or

ii)

G
has a
k
-
(\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}}(t,k),\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4),\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}}(t,k),r)
-
\Sigma
-layout
\Lambda
centred at
M
where
\Sigma
is a surface
where
H
does not embed
and
\delta
is
(M-A)
-central where
A
is the apex set of
\Lambda
.

Moreover, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}}(t,k),~\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}}(t,k)\in\mathbf{O}\big((t+k)^{112}\big),\text{ and }\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}}(t,r,k)\in\mathbf{O}\big((t+k)^{115}r\big).\end{aligned}

There also exists an algorithm that, given
t,k,r
, a graph
H
, a graph
G
and a mesh
M
as above as input finds one of these outcomes in time
\mathbf{poly}(t+k+r)|E(G)||V(G)|^{2}
.

In the following we first present a proof of a slightly weaker statement, namely
theorem

15.4
.
The proof can easily be adapted to a proof of
theorem

15.1
as explained in
section

15.4
.

15.1
Extending the surface

In order to be able to extend our surface whenever we find a crosscap or handle transaction, we require a way to synchronise this crosscap or handle with the surface we are currently on.
So far, all of our tools have been tailored to make our graph similar to the surface as possible.
This time around the surface has to follow the lead of our graph.

We import two crucial lemmas from Kawarabayashi et al.
[
KTW21
]
which are strong enough to not require any additional refinement.
Indeed, their lemmas are already exactly in the format we need them to be.
Since both lemmas are long and rather technical to state we dedicate this section purely to their statement and some explanation.

(G,\Omega)
X_{1}
X_{2}
c_{1}^{\prime}
c_{1}
\mathcal{P}
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
\mathcal{R}
\mathcal{C}
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}

Figure 49
:
The outcome of
proposition

15.2
when applied to the society
(G,\Omega)
with nest
\mathcal{C}
and radial linkage
\mathcal{R}
. The purple overlay indicates how the nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
surrounds the vortex
c_{1}^{\prime}
in the cylindrical rendition of the vortex society of
c_{1}
.

Proposition 15.2

(Kawarabayashi, Thomas, and Wollan
[
KTW21
]
)
.

Let
s
and
p
be non-negative integers.
Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with a cylindrical rendition
\rho_{0}
in the disk
\Delta
with a nest
\mathcal{C}=\{C_{1},\dots,C_{s+9}\}
around the vortex
c_{0}
.
Let
X_{1},X_{2}
be disjoint segments of
\Omega
such that there exist

•

a radial linkage
\mathcal{R}
orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}
starting in
X_{1}
, and

•

a flat crosscap transaction
\mathcal{P}
of order at least
p+2s+7
with all endpoints in
X_{2}
and disjoint from
\mathcal{R}
.

Let
\Sigma^{*}
be a surface, homeomorphic to the projective plane minus an open disk, which is obtained from
\Delta
by adding a crosscap to the interior of
c_{0}
.

Then there exists a crosscap transaction
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
of order
p
, consisting of the middle
p
paths of
\mathcal{P}
, and a rendition
\rho_{1}
of
(G,\Omega)
in
\Sigma^{*}
with a unique vortex
c_{1}
and the following hold:

i)

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is disjoint from
\sigma(c_{1})
,

ii)

the vortex society of
c_{1}
in
\rho_{1}
has a cylindrical rendition
\rho_{1}^{\prime}
with a nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=\{C_{1}^{\prime},\dots,C_{s}^{\prime}\}
around the unique vortex
c_{1}^{\prime}
,

iii)

every element of
\mathcal{P}
has an endpoint in
V(\sigma_{\rho_{1}^{\prime}}(c_{1}^{\prime}))
,

iv)

\mathcal{R}
is orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and for every
i\in[s]
and every
R\in\mathcal{R}
,
C_{i}^{\prime}\cap R=C_{i+8}\cap R
. Moreover,

v)

let
\mathcal{R}=\{R_{1},\dots,R_{\ell}\}
. For each
i\in[\ell]
let
x_{i}
be the endpoint of
R_{i}
in
X_{1}
, and let
y_{i}
be the last vertex of
R_{i}
on
c_{1}
when traversing along
R_{i}
starting from
x_{i}
; then if
x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{\ell}
appear in
\Omega
in the order listed, then
y_{1},y_{2},\dots,y_{\ell}
appear on
\widetilde{c_{1}}
in the order listed.

vi)

Finally, let
\Delta^{\prime}
be the open disk bounded by the trace of
C_{s+8}
in
\rho_{0}
.
Then
\rho_{0}
restricted to
\Delta\setminus\Delta^{\prime}
is equal to
\rho_{1}
restricted to
\Delta\setminus\Delta^{\prime}
.

Moreover, there exists an algorithm that computes this outcome in time
\mathbf{poly}(sp|\mathcal{R}|)|V(G)|
.

To summarise,
proposition

15.2
allows to isolate some part of a flat crosscap transaction and to pull it ”inside” the already embedded part of the graph.
By doing so, the lemma cuts out a disk from the initial disk
\Delta
, then glues in a Moebius-strip, and finally cuts out another disk to allow for a new vortex together with its nest.
This newly created society which contains the new nest and vortex is guaranteed to be fully disjoint from the “protected” part of the crosscap transaction that was pulled in.
Moreover, the lemma allows to propagate a radial linkage through the newly added Moebius-strip.
These are the key features that will allow for the construction of our surface-wall later on.
See
figure

49
for an illustration.

Please note that the original statement of
proposition

15.2
in
[
KTW21
]
requires
\mathcal{R}
to end on the boundary of the vortex
c_{0}
but only needs
s+8
cycles in the initial nest instead of
s+9
.
By requiring one additional cycle we do not need
\mathcal{R}
to extend all the way to
c_{0}
since we can declare the
c_{0}
-disk of the innermost cycle to be the vortex.
This, however, costs one cycle which is taken account for by the slight increase of the number of required cycles in the nest.
This also explains why we shift, in iv), by
+8
instead of
+7
as in the original statement.

Next we introduce the analogue of
proposition

15.2
for handle transactions.
While the statement is somewhat different, the idea remains the same.

(G,\Omega)
X_{1}
X_{2}
c_{1}^{\prime}
c_{1}
\mathcal{P}_{1}
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}
\mathcal{P}_{2}
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
\mathcal{R}

:

15.3
when applied to the society

. The purple overlay indicates how the nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
surrounds the vortex

in the cylindrical rendition of the vortex society of

.

Proposition 15.3

(Kawarabayashi, Thomas, and Wollan
[
KTW21
]
)
.

Let
s
and
p
be non-negative integers.
Let
(G,\Omega)
be a society with a cylindrical rendition
\rho_{0}
in the disk
\Delta
with a nest
\mathcal{C}=\{C_{1},\dots,C_{s+9}\}
around the vortex
c_{0}
.
Let
X_{1},X_{2}
be disjoint segments of
\Omega
such that there exist

•

a radial linkage
\mathcal{R}
orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}
starting in

, and

•

a flat handle transaction
\mathcal{P}
of order at least

with all endpoints in

and disjoint from
\mathcal{R}
.

Let
\mathcal{P}_{1}
and

be the two planar transactions such that
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}_{1}\cup\mathcal{P}_{2}
.
Let
\Sigma^{+}
be a surface, homeomorphic to the torus minus an open disk, which is obtained from
\Delta
by adding a handle to the interior of
c_{0}
.

Then there exist transactions
\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{1}
and
\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{P}_{2}
of order
p
, each

consisting of the middle

paths of
\mathcal{P}_{i}
,
i\in[2]
, such that
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}_{1}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{P}_{2}^{\prime}
is a handle transaction, and a rendition
\rho_{1}
of
(G,\Omega)
in
\Sigma^{+}
with a unique vortex
c_{1}
and the following hold:

i)

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
is disjoint from
\sigma(c_{1})
,

ii)

the vortex society of
c_{1}
in
\rho_{1}
has a cylindrical rendition
\rho_{1}^{\prime}
with a nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}=\{C_{1}^{\prime},\dots,C_{s}^{\prime}\}
around the unique vortex
c_{1}^{\prime}
,

iii)

every element of
\mathcal{P}
has an endpoint in
V(\sigma_{\rho_{1}^{\prime}}(c_{1}^{\prime}))
,

\mathcal{R}
is orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and for every
i\in[s]
and every

,
C_{i}^{\prime}\cap R=C_{i+8}\cap R
. Moreover,

v)

let
\mathcal{R}=\{R_{1},\dots,R_{\ell}\}
. For each
i\in[\ell]
let
x_{i}
be the endpoint of
R_{i}
in
X_{1}
, and let
y_{i}
be the last vertex of
R_{i}
on
c_{1}
when traversing along
R_{i}
starting from
x_{i}
; then if
x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{\ell}
appear in

in the order listed, then
y_{1},y_{2},\dots,y_{\ell}
appear on
\widetilde{c_{1}}
in the order listed.

Finally, let
\Delta^{\prime}
be the open disk bounded by the trace of
C_{s+8}
in
\rho_{0}
.
Then
\rho_{0}
restricted to

is equal to
\rho_{1}
restricted to

.

Moreover, there exists an algorithm that computes this outcome in time
\mathbf{poly}(sp|\mathcal{R}|)|V(G)|
.

.
As before, our statement of
proposition

15.3
is slightly changed when compared to its original form as stated by Kawarabayashi et al.
[
KTW21
]
.
Again, we increase the size of the initial nest from
s+8
by one to
s+9
to not have to maintain that our radial linkage reaches all the way to the vortex
c_{0}
.

15.2
The proof of the Local Structure Theorem

We proceed with a proof of a first version of the Local Structure Theorem with polynomial bounds.

Theorem 15.4
.

There exist functions
\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}},\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{2}\to\mathbb{N}
and
\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{3}\to\mathbb{N}
such that for all non-negative integers
k
,
t\geq 5
, and
r
, every graph
G
and every
\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,r,k)
mesh
M\subseteq G
one of the following holds.

i)

G
has a
K_{t}
-minor controlled by
M
, or

ii)

G
has a
k
-
(\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t),\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4),\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k),r)
-
\Sigma
-layout centred at
M
where
\Sigma
is a surface of Euler-genus at most
t^{2}
.

Moreover, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k),~\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k)\in\mathbf{O}\big((t+k)^{112}\big),\text{ and }\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,r,k)\in\mathbf{O}\big((t+k)^{115}+t^{3}r\big).\end{aligned}

There also exists an algorithm that, given
t,k,r
, a graph
G
and a mesh
M
as above as input finds one of these outcomes in time
\mathbf{poly}(t+k+r)|E(G)||V(G)|^{2}
.

Note that, while
theorem

15.4
is already strong enough to prove the conjecture of Kawarabayashi, Thomas, and Wollan for polynomial bounds for the GMST, the bound on the Euler-genus it guarantees is not optimal.
Moreover, it is only concerned with clique-minors rather than arbitrary graphs
H
.
A more refined and even stronger version of the Local Structure Theorem, namely the main theorem of this section, can be found in
theorem

15.1
.
In
section

15.4
we explain how to adapt the proof of
theorem

15.4
to derive
theorem

15.1
.
Here, we also implement tight bounds on the Euler-genus.

Let us begin by providing estimates for the functions mentioned in
theorem

15.4
.
For this, we first need to fix four intermediate values as follows.
Let
\mathsf{c}
be the constant from
corollary

5.8
such that every
x
-surface-wall
W
with
h
handles and
c
crosscaps where
2h+c=t^{2}
and
x=\mathsf{c}t^{12}
contains a
K_{t}
-minor controlled by the cylindrical wall of
W
.
The following functions determine the order of the transactions and the order of the nests we will need to find throughout the proof.
Both functions depend on a parameter
g
we use to keep track on the number of “genus-increasing” steps we have performed in order to facilitate an inductive proof.

\displaystyle\mathsf{radial}(g,t,k)\coloneqq~

\displaystyle(g+2)(8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}+4+\nicefrac{{4k}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)+1)

\displaystyle\mathsf{nest}(g,t,k)\coloneqq~

\displaystyle(g+2)\big(8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}+\mathsf{radial}(g-1,t,k)+14+\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))

\displaystyle+\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)\big)+\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))+\nicefrac{{4k}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)+1

\displaystyle\mathsf{transaction}(g,t,k)\coloneqq~

\displaystyle 4k+4\mathsf{c}t^{12}+24+4\mathsf{nest}(g-1,t,k)+2\mathsf{radial}(g-1,t,k)

With these values fixed, the functions of
theorem

15.4
are as follows.

\displaystyle\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k)\coloneqq~

\displaystyle t^{2}\cdot\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)+\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{fin}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1),\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2},t,k))+16t^{3}

\displaystyle\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k)\coloneqq~

\displaystyle\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1),\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2},t,k))

\displaystyle\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,r,k)\coloneqq~

\displaystyle 100t^{3}\Big(\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k)+2\mathsf{nest}(t^{2},t,k)+2t+17+r

\displaystyle+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)(2\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1),\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2},t,k))+1)\Big)

Using the bounds provided in the proof of
Theorem

14.1
, we can give the following estimates for the functions above:
We have
\mathsf{radial}(g,t,k)\in\mathbf{O}(gk+gt^{12})
and
\mathsf{nest}(g,t,k),\mathsf{transaction}(g,t,k)\in\mathbf{O}(t^{20}+t^{12}g^{2}+t^{8}k+g^{2}k)
.
This in turn allows us to determine that
\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k)\in\mathbf{O}((t+k)^{112})
,
\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k)\in\mathbf{O}(t+k)^{107}
, and
\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,r,k)\in\mathbf{O}((t+k)^{115}+t^{3}r)
.

Proof of
theorem

15.4
.

Let
M
be a
\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k,r)
-mesh in
G
.
We begin by applying
theorem

6.2
to

.
This yields, in time

-minor controlled by

or we find a set

of size at most

and an
x
-submesh

of

with

\displaystyle x\geq~

\displaystyle\frac{1}{100t^{3}}\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k,r)-2t-4

\displaystyle\geq~

\displaystyle\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k)+9+r+2\mathsf{nest}(t^{2},t,k)

\displaystyle+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)(2\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1),\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2},t,k))+1)

Let
\mathcal{C}_{0}^{\prime}=\{{C^{0}}^{\prime}_{1},\dots,{C^{0}}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{nest}(t^{2},t,k)}\}

of the

.

.

Inductively refining an near embedding: The set-up.

Observe that the tuple
\Lambda_{0}=(A_{0},M_{0},D_{0},\delta_{0})
is, in fact, a
\Sigma_{0}
-landscape of detail
4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1)

(4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))
-surface-wall without handles or crosscaps whose base cycles coincide with the cycles in

, this wall exists as a consequence of

.

We now prove that that given the following collection of objects for some
i\in[0,t^{2}-1]
, we can apply

to the society

-layout centred at

Since we have already shown how to reach the point

comes true.

For now, let us introduce the objects we are looking for.
Let

•

with

•

a cozy nest

all objects above are chosen such that

Inductively refining an near embedding: Construction

So suppose for some
i\in[0,t^{2}-1]
the list of objects as above has already been constructed.

We begin by applying
theorem

14.1
to
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
.
What follows is a case distinction on the different possible outcomes.

Case 1:
The outcome is
i)
of
theorem

14.1
which yields a
K_{t}
-minor controlled, and therefore controlled, by a mesh whose horizontal paths are subpaths of distinct cycles of
\mathcal{C}_{i}
.
It is easy to see that this implies that this

-minor is controlled by
M_{0}
as desired.

Case 2:

Let
(G^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime})
be the
C^{i}_{\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-1}
-society in
\rho_{i}
.
Case 2 contains two subcases that can be treated in almost identical ways.
Those are outcome
ii)
and outcome

of
theorem

14.1
where the both outcomes yield a set

of size at most
\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)
.

Moreover, in the first outcome
\mathcal{Q}
is a flat crosscap transaction of order
\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2}-i,t,k)
in
(G^{\prime}-A,\Omega^{\prime})
together with a nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
\rho_{i}
of order
\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)
around

to which

is orthogonal.

In the second outcome,
\mathcal{Q}

in

together with a nest
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
\rho_{i}
of order
\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)
around

to which

and

are orthogonal.

In both cases we can reduce the order of

by

by shedding off, in case one the last

and in case two, from each

,
