, the last
\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)
.
Let
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{2}
, be the resulting transactions.
Moreover, notice that we may select a radial linkage
\mathcal{L}
of order
\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)
in
(G^{\prime}-A,\Omega^{\prime})
which is orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
Indeed, we may select
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}
such that there are segments
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
of
\Omega^{\prime}
where
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
has all endpoints in
V(I_{1})
,
\mathcal{L}
has its endpoints in
V(\Omega^{\prime})
in
V(I_{2})
, and
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
are disjoint.
It follows that
|\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}|=4k+4\mathsf{c}t^{12}+14+4\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)
and
|\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{\prime}|=|\mathcal{Q}_{2}^{\prime}|=2k+2\mathsf{c}t^{12}+7+2\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)
in case
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
is a handle transaction.

In the following we discuss only how to proceed in the case where
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
is a crosscap transaction.
The second case, namely the one where
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
is a handle transactions, can be handles analogously with the exception that, instead of
proposition

15.2
one needs to use
proposition

15.3
and some of the choices we make below need to be made for the transactions
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{i}
and the objects derived from them individually.
Upon closer inspection, the reader will see that the size of
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
(and in particular of
\mathcal{Q}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}^{\prime}
) was chosen to allow the application of any of these two lemmas.

Let us assume
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}=\{Q_{1},\dots,Q_{4k+4\mathsf{c}t^{12}+14+4\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)}\}
to be numbered such that
Q_{j}
separates
Q_{j-1}
from
Q_{j+1}
within the strip of
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
for all
j\in[2,4k+4\mathsf{c}t^{12}+14+4\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)-1]
.
Let
a\coloneqq 2\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)+7
and
b\coloneqq 2\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)+4k+4\mathsf{c}t^{12}+8
.
Moreover, let
\mathcal{A}\coloneqq\{Q_{1},\dots,A_{a-1}\}
,
\mathcal{B}=\{Q_{b+1},\dots,Q_{4k+4\mathsf{c}t^{12}+14+4\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)}\}
, and
\mathcal{I}\coloneqq\{Q_{a},\dots,Q_{b}\}
.

We assume the cycles in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
to be numbered
C_{1}^{\prime},\dots,C_{\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)}^{\prime}
from innermost to outermost.
Let
I=[\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)-3,\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)-8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}-\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)-9]\subseteq[\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)]
.
Then
|I|=8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}+\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)+6=2|\mathcal{I}|+|\mathcal{L}|+2
.
Observe that this means, in particular,

\displaystyle 2|\mathcal{I}|+|\mathcal{L}|=~

\displaystyle 8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}+4+\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)\leq\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i,t,k)=|\mathcal{R}_{i}|.

Let us now select
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
to be all
V(\Omega^{\prime})
-
V(C_{1}^{\prime})
-subpaths of the paths in
\mathcal{I}
.
It follows that
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
is a radial linkage of order
2|\mathcal{I}|
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}
is a radial linkage of order
8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}+\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)+4=|I|-2
which is orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
This allows us to call
lemma

10.1
for
\mathcal{R}_{i}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}
,
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, and
I
.
As a result we obtain, in time
\mathbf{poly}(t+k)|E(G)|
, a radial linkage
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
of order
|I|-2
which shares its endpoints on
V(\Omega^{\prime})
with the endpoints of
\mathcal{R}_{i}
and its endpoints on
C_{1}^{\prime}
with
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
is orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\setminus\{C_{i}~\colon~i\in I\}
and within the inner graph, let us call it
G^{\prime\prime}
, of
C_{\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)-8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}-\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)-9}^{\prime}
it is disjoint from
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{I}
.
Let
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
be all those paths that do not meet
\mathcal{I}
within
G^{\prime\prime}
.
Then
|\mathcal{J}|=|\mathcal{R}^{\prime}|-8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}-4=\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)
.

Next we inspect our remaining nest a bit closer.
Let
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
be the set of the innermost
\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)-8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}-\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)-10
cycles of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
Let
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
be the cyclic ordering of the ground vertices of
C^{\prime}_{\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)-8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}-\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)-9}
and let
(G^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime})
the the resulting society with cylindrical rendition
\rho^{\prime\prime}
, which is the restriction of
\rho^{\prime}
to the
c_{i}
-disk
\Delta^{\prime}
in
\Delta_{i}
bounded by the trace of
C^{\prime}_{\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)-8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}-\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)-9}
.
Moreover, let
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}
be the restrictions of
\mathcal{J}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
to
G^{\prime\prime}
.

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}|=~

\displaystyle\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)-8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}

\displaystyle-\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)-10

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle(t^{2}-i-1)\big(8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}+\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i,t,k)+24+\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))+\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)\big)

\displaystyle+\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))+\nicefrac{{4k}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)+15

\displaystyle\geq~

\displaystyle(t^{2}-i-1)\big(8k+8\mathsf{c}t^{12}+\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)+24+\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))+\mathsf{loss}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)\big)

\displaystyle+\mathsf{nest}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))+\nicefrac{{4k}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)+15

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1)+14

and

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}|=~

\displaystyle 4k+4\mathsf{c}t^{12}+14+4\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle 2(2k+2\mathsf{c}t^{12})+4\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)+14

Notice that the calculations above suffice for the application of
proposition

15.2
.
Indeed, we exceed the necessary numbers by more than
2\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)
.
If instead we were dealing with a handle transaction we would next want to apply
proposition

15.3
.
Here we would have constructed two transactions, namely
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
, each of order at least
2k+2\mathsf{c}t^{12}+2\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)+7
and thus, together, they form a transaction
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}
of the order above.
This explains out choice for the size of
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}
.

So we are now ready to apply
proposition

15.2
to
(G^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime})
,
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}
, and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}
with
p=4k+4\mathsf{c}t^{12}+2
, rendition
\rho^{\prime}
and the disk
\Delta^{\prime}
.
As a result we obtain the following list of objects.
Let
\Sigma^{*}
be a surface homeomorphic to the projective plane minus an open disk which is obtained from
\Delta^{\prime}
by adding a crosscap to the interior of
c_{i}
.
Then there exists
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}
which is exactly the restriction of
\mathcal{I}
to
G^{\prime\prime}
, and a rendition
\rho^{\prime\prime\prime}
of
(G^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime})
in
\Sigma^{*}
with a unique vortex
c_{i+1}^{\prime}
and the following properties hold:

•

\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
is disjoint from
\sigma(c_{i+1}^{\prime})
,

•

the vortex society
(G_{i+1},\Omega_{i+1})
of
c_{i+1}^{\prime}
in
\rho^{\prime\prime\prime}
has a cylindrical rendition
\rho_{i+1}
with nest
\mathcal{C}_{i+1}=\{C_{1}^{i+1},\dots,C^{i+1}_{\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i-1)}\}
around the unique vortex
c_{i+1}
,

•

every element of
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime\prime}
has an endpoint in
V(\sigma_{\rho_{i+1}(c_{i+1})})
, and

•

\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}
is orthogonal to
\mathcal{C}_{i+1}
. Moreover,

•

let
\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}=\{J_{1},\dots,J_{\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)}\}
.
For each
j\in[\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)]
let
x_{j}
be the endpoint of
J_{j}
in
V(\Omega^{\prime\prime})
and let
y_{j}
be the endpoint of
J_{j}
on
c_{i+1}^{\prime}
; then if
x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)}
appear on
\Omega^{\prime\prime}
in the order listed, then
y_{1},y_{2},\dots,y_{\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k)}
appear on
\widetilde{c}_{i+1}^{\prime}
in the order listed.

•

Finally, let
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
be the open disk bounded by the trace of of the outermost cycle of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
in
\rho^{\prime}
.
Then
\rho^{\prime}
restricted to
\Delta^{\prime}\setminus\Delta^{\prime\prime}
is equal to
\rho^{\prime\prime\prime}
restricted to
\Delta^{\prime}\setminus\Delta^{\prime\prime}
.

Now let
\delta_{i+1}
be obtained by first unifying the renditions
\rho^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\rho_{i+1}
along the vortex society of
c_{i+1}^{\prime}
, then combining the resulting rendition of
(G^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime})
in the disk
\Delta^{\prime}
with the rendition
\rho^{\prime}
along to boundary of
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
, and then reintegrating
\rho^{\prime}
into
\delta_{i}
.
Moreover, let
\Sigma_{i+1}
be obtained from
\Sigma_{i}
be first removing the interior of
\Delta^{\prime}
and replacing it with
\Sigma^{*}
.
Notice that this means that
\Sigma_{i+1}
is obtained from
\Sigma_{i}
by adding a single crosscap.
We also set
A_{i+1}\coloneqq A_{i}\cup A
and obtain

\displaystyle|A_{i+1}|\leq~

\displaystyle i\cdot\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)+16t^{3}+\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle(i+1)\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)+16t^{3}.

So the first three points of our invariant are maintained.

Notice that, by construction, we have that the radial linkage
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
can be extended onto the restriction of
\mathcal{R}_{i}
to the outer graph of the closed curve obtained by following along the vertices on
\Omega^{\prime}
.
This allows us to, firstly, extend the crosscap transaction
\mathcal{I}
of order
4k+4\mathsf{c}t^{12}+2
along
\mathcal{R}_{i}
to obtain a crosscap transaction
\mathcal{P}_{i+1}
on
(G_{0},\Omega_{0})
which is disjoint from
\mathcal{P}_{j}
,
j\in[i]
,
secondly, to extend
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
along
\mathcal{R}_{i}
to form the radial linkage
\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
which is orthogonal to both
\mathcal{C}^{*}
and
\mathcal{C}_{i+1}
and satisfies

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}_{i+1}|=\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i-1,t,k),

its endpoints coincide with some of the endpoints of
\mathcal{R}_{0}
, and it is disjoint from the paths in
\mathfrak{P}_{i+1}=\mathfrak{P}_{i}\cup\{\mathcal{P}_{i+1}\}
.
Indeed, it is straight forward to see that
(G_{0}-A_{i+1},\Omega_{0},\mathcal{C}^{*},\,\mathcal{R}_{i+1},\mathfrak{P}_{i+1})
is a
\Sigma_{i+1}
-configuration of strength
(4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1),\mathsf{radial}(t^{2}-i,t,k),p_{1},\dots,p_{i+1})
with
p_{j}=4k+4\mathsf{c}t^{12}
.
So the last three points of our invariant are also satisfied.

By
observation

5.4
the
\Sigma_{i+1}
-configuration found in the previous paragraph yields the existence of a
(4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))
-surface wall
D_{i+1}
with the amount of crosscaps and handles used to obtain
\Sigma_{i+1}
from the sphere such that the base cycles of
D_{i+1}
coincide with the cycles of
\mathcal{C}^{*}
.
This, together with the society
(G_{i+1},\Omega_{i+1})
, the cylindrical rendition
\rho_{i+1}
and the nest
\mathcal{C}_{i+1}
around the unique vortex
c_{i+1}
of both
\rho_{i+1}
and
\delta_{i+1}
, satisfies the remaining three points of our invariant and thus this step is complete.

Observe that, in the case where
i=t^{2}-1
we have reached a situation where
K_{t}
embeds in
\Sigma_{i+1}
.
This means that
corollary

5.8
implies the existence of a
K_{t}
-model controlled by
D_{i+1}
.
So in this case we would be done immediately.
Hence, we may assume that
i+1\in[t^{2}-1]
.

Case 3:

The remaining case to be discussed is outcome
iv)
of
theorem

14.1
.
Here we are given a set
A\subseteq V(G_{i})
with
|A|\leq\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1),\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2},t,k))
, a rendition
\rho^{\prime}
of
(G_{i}-A,\Omega_{i})
in
\Delta_{i}
with breadth
b\in[\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)-1]
and depth at most
\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1),\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2}-i,t,k))
, and an extended
(4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))
-Dyck-wall
D
with signature
(0,0,b)
, such that
D
is grounded in
\rho^{\prime}
, the base cycles of
D
are the cycles
C_{\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-1-k-\mathsf{c}t^{12}}^{i},\dots,C_{\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-1}^{i}
, and there exists a bijection between the vortices
v
of
\rho^{\prime}
and the vortex segments
S_{v}
of
D
, where
v
is the unique vortex contained in the disk
\Delta_{S_{v}}
defined by the trace of the inner cycle of the nest of
S_{v}
where
\Delta_{S_{v}}
is chosen to avoid the trace of the simple cycle of
D
.

Notice that the union of all rails of
D
form a radial linkage
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}
of order
16b(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12})
such that every vortex segment provides exactly
16(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12})
of these paths.
Moreover, there are at precisely
4b(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12})+4
cycles among
C_{\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-1-k-\mathsf{c}t^{12}}^{i},\dots,C_{\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-1}^{i}
.
Now let
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
be a radial linkage of order
4b(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12})
formed by selecting
4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1)
rails from each vortex segment of
D
.

Notice that, by definition of
D
, for every
j\in[\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-1-k-\mathsf{c}t^{12},\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-1]
and every vortex
v
of
\rho
, both the nest of
S_{v}
and
v
itself are disjoint from
C^{i}_{j}
.

Let
I=[\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-1-k-\mathsf{c}t^{12}+2,\mathsf{nest}(t^{2}-i,t,k)-\mathsf{cost}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1))-1]]
.
Then we have
|I|=4b(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12})+2
.
Therefore, we may call upon
lemma

10.1
for
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
,
I
,
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{R}_{i}
to obtain a radial linkage
\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
of order
4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12})
whose endpoints on the outermost cycles of the nests of the
S_{v}
coincide with the vertices of the rails of
S_{v}
for every vortex segment
S_{v}
of
\rho^{\prime}
and whose other endpoints coincide with the endpoints of
\mathcal{R}_{i}
on
V(\Omega_{0})
.
Moreover, the endpoints of the paths among
\mathcal{R}_{i}
that lead to
S_{v}
appear consecutively on
\Omega_{0}
.
Hence, we may obtain an extended
k
-surface wall
D_{i+1}
from
D_{i}
by discarding some of the cycles and paths in each of the handle and crosscap segments and integrating the nests of the
S_{v}
along the paths in
\mathcal{R}_{i+1}
.
We may also obtain a
\Sigma_{i}
-decomposition
\delta_{i+1}
of breadth at most
b
and depth at most

\displaystyle\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1),\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2}-i,t,k))\leq\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1),\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2},t,k))

for
G-A_{i+1}
where
A_{i+1\coloneqq A_{i}\cup A}
with

\displaystyle|A_{i+1}|\leq~

\displaystyle|A_{i}|+\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1),\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2},t,k))

\displaystyle\leq~

\displaystyle i\cdot\mathsf{apex}^{\mathsf{genus}}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t)+\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,4(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12}+1),\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2},t,k))+16t^{3}.

It follows, in particular from the choice of the size of
M_{0}
, that
\Lambda=(A_{i+1},M_{0},D_{i+1},\delta_{i})
is a
k
-
(\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t^{2},t,k),\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4),\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k),r)
-
\Sigma_{i}
-layout centred at
M
as desired.
With this, our proof is complete.
∎

15.3
Structure with respect to a mesh

The
\Sigma
-decomposition, or better the
\Sigma
-layout of
G
we obtain from
theorem

15.4
is “
centred at
M
” where
M
is the mesh we start with.
This property is, at least at first glance, different from the property Robertson and Seymour
[
RS03b
]
, and later Kawarabayashi et al.
[
KTW21
]
, established in their versions of the Local Structure Theorem.
They were looking for a property called “
W
-central
” where
W
is a large wall.
In this subsection we establish that the
\Sigma
-layouts provided by
theorem

15.4
also imply the property of being
M
-central, where we lift the definition of
W
-central of Robertson and Seymour to arbitrary meshes instead of restricting ourselves to walls.

For this, recall that, given some
r
-mesh
M
and a separation
(A,B)
of order at most
r-1
we said that
X\in\{A,B\}
is the
majority side
of
(A,B)
if
X\setminus Y
, where
Y\in\{A,B\}\setminus\{X\}
, contains the vertices of both a vertical and a horizontal path of
M
.
Moreover, we observed that the orientation
\mathcal{T}_{M}
of
\mathcal{S}_{r}
is a tangle.

Let
\Sigma
be a surface and let
\delta
be a
\Sigma
-decomposition of a graph
G
containing an
r
-mesh
M
.
We say that
\delta
is
M
-central
if there is no cell
c\in C(\delta)
such that
V(\sigma(c))
contains the majority side of a separation from
\mathcal{T}_{M}
.
Similarly, let
A\subseteq V(G)
,
|A|\leq r-1
, let
\Sigma^{\prime}
be a surface and
\delta^{\prime}
be a
\Sigma^{\prime}
-decomposition of
G-A
.
Then we say that
\delta^{\prime}
is
(M-A)
-central
for
G
if no cell of
\delta^{\prime}
contains the majority side of a separation from
\mathcal{T}_{M}\cap\mathcal{S}_{r-|A|}
.

The property of being
M
-central for a mesh can be stated more abstractly, fully with respect to a tangle and not a mesh.
That is, let
\mathcal{T}
be a tangle of order
r
in a graph
G
,let
\Sigma
be a surface, and
\delta
be a
\Sigma
-decomposition for
G
.
We say that
\delta
is
\mathcal{T}
-central
if no cell of
\delta
contains the big side of a separation from
\mathcal{T}
.
Given a set
A\subseteq V(G)
,
|A|\leq r-1
, a surface
\Sigma^{\prime}
and a
\Sigma^{\prime}
-decomposition
\delta^{\prime}
for
G-A
, we say that
\delta^{\prime}
is
(\mathcal{T}-A)
-central
if no cell of
\delta^{\prime}
contains the big side of a separation from
\mathcal{T}\cap\mathcal{S}_{r-|A|}
.

In
[
KTW21
]
the authors prove the following useful result.
The version of their lemma, namely Lemma 15.3, we state here is slightly enhanced as it also contains the additional information proven in Claim 1 of the proof of Lemma 15.3 in
[
KTW21
]
.

Proposition 15.7

(Kawarabayashi, Thomas, and Wollan
[
KTW21
]
)
.

Let
b,d,r\geq 0
be integers with
r\geq b(2d+1)+6
.
Let
G
be a graph,
\Sigma
be a surface and
\delta
be a
\Sigma
-decomposition for
G
of breadth at most
b
and depth at most
d
.
Moreover, let
M
be an
r
-mesh in
G
which is flat in
\delta
.
Finally, let
c_{1},\dots,c_{b}\in C(\delta)
be the vortices of
\delta
and, for each
i\in[b]
, let
(G_{i},\Omega_{i})
be the vortex society of
c_{i}
.

Then there exists a set
A\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in[b]}V(G_{i})
of size at most
b(2d+1)
with
|A\cap V(G_{i})|\leq 2d+1
such that, for every tangle
\mathcal{T}
with
\mathcal{T}_{M}\subseteq\mathcal{T}
, the restriction of
\delta
to
G-A
is
(\mathcal{T}-A)
-central.

Moreover, there exists an algorithm that finds such a set
A
, given
G
,
\delta
, and
M
, in time
\mathbf{poly}(r)|E(G)|
.

Now
proposition

15.7
allows us to strengthen
theorem

15.4
by ensuring that the
\Sigma
-decomposition found is also
M
-central where
M
is the initial mesh.

Theorem 15.8
.

There exist functions
\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:stronger_localstructure}},\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:stronger_localstructure}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{2}\to\mathbb{N}
and
\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:stronger_localstructure}}\colon\mathbb{N}^{3}\to\mathbb{N}
such that for all non-negative integers
k
,
t\geq 5
, and
r
, every graph
G
and every
\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:stronger_localstructure}}(t,r,k)
mesh
M\subseteq G
one of the following holds.

i)

G
has a
K_{t}
-minor controlled by
M
, or

ii)

G
has a
k
-
(\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:stronger_localstructure}}(t),\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4),\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:stronger_localstructure}}(t,k),r)
-
\Sigma
-layout
\Lambda
centred at
M
where
\Sigma
is a surface of Euler-genus at most
t^{2}
and
\delta
is
(M-A)
-central where
A
is the apex set of
\Lambda
.

Moreover, it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:stronger_localstructure}}(t,k),~\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:stronger_localstructure}}(t,k)\in\mathbf{O}\big((t+k)^{112}\big),\text{ and }\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:stronger_localstructure}}(t,r,k)\in\mathbf{O}\big((t+k)^{115}r\big).\end{aligned}

There also exists an algorithm that, given
t,k,r
, a graph
G
and a mesh
M
as above as input finds one of these outcomes in time
\mathbf{poly}(t+k+r)|E(G)||V(G)|^{2}
.

Proof.

This strengthening follows immediately from
theorem

15.4
by applying
proposition

15.7
after making the following observations.

First, if the outcome of
theorem

15.4
is the clique-minor, there is nothing to show.

Now let
\Lambda
be the
k
-
(\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t),\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4),\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k),r)
-
\Sigma
-layout with mesh
M_{0}
and
\Sigma
-decomposition
\delta
.
By definition of
\Sigma
-layouts, we know that
M_{0}
is a
\big(\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:localstructure}}(t,k)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)(2\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k+\mathsf{c}t^{12},\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2},t,k))+1)+11+r\big)
-submesh of
M
and thus
\mathcal{T}_{M_{0}}\subseteq\mathcal{T}_{M}
.

Finally, let
A_{1}
be the apex set of
\Lambda
and notice that
V(M_{0})\cap A_{1}=\emptyset
.
This implies that the restriction of
\mathcal{T}_{M_{0}}
to
G-A_{1}
is still a tangle of order
\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}(t-3)(t-4)(2\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:societyclassification}}(t,k+\mathsf{c}t^{12},\mathsf{transaction}(t^{2},t,k))+1)+11+r
.

If we now let
A_{2}
be the set produced by
proposition

15.7
we may simply set
A\coloneqq A_{1}\cup A_{2}
and the proof is complete.
∎

15.4
Two conjectures of Wollan

In his survey on explicit bounds on the GMST, Wollan states two conjectures regarding the Local Structure Theorem
[
Wol22
]
.
The first one asks if a Local Structure Theorem with polynomial bounds is possible while also having a tight bound on the Euler-genus of the surfaces that may occur.
This conjecture reads as follows.

Any large mesh naturally gives rise to a model
\eta
of a grid.
Let
H
and
G
be graphs with
t=|V(H)|
.
We say that a mesh
M

grasps
a model
\mu
of
H
if for every branch set
\mu(v)
,
v\in V(H)
, there exist distinct indices
r_{1},\dots,r_{t}
of the rows of
M
and distinct indices
c_{1},\dots,c_{t}
of the columns of
M
such that
\mu(v)
contains the intersection of the
r_{i}
th row and
c_{i}
th column of
M
of for all
i\in[t]
.

Notice that any minor model that is grasped by a large mesh is also controlled by it.
The reverse, however, is not immediately true.
Kawarabayshi, Thomas, and Wollen proved their variant of the Local Structure Theorem in terms of
K_{t}
-minors being
grasped
rather than controlled because they sought a way to avoid the use of tangles in their proofs
[
Wol25
]
.
Since their theorems were stated using this notion, it is natural that Wollan stated his conjectures also for the notion of a minor being grasped.

Conjecture 15.9

(Wollan
[
Wol22
]
)
.

There exist polynomials
p,q
which satisfy the following.
Let
r,t\geq 0
be integers and let
H
be a graph with
t
vertices.
Let
R=p(t,r)+q(t)
.
Let
G
be a graph and
W
be an
R
-wall in
G
.

Then either
G
has a model of an
H
-minor grasped by
W
, or there exists a subset
A
of at most
q(t)
vertex of
G
, a surface
\Sigma
in which
H
does not embed and a
W
-central
\Sigma
-decomposition
\delta
of
G-A
of depth and breadth at most
q(t)
and an
r
-subwall
W^{\prime}
of
W
such that
V(W^{\prime})\cap A=\emptyset
and
W^{\prime}
is flat in
\delta
.

His second conjecture is a special case of
conjecture

15.9
for graphs that embed in bounded genus surfaces.
He claims that
conjecture

15.9
would imply the following.

Let
G
be a graph drawn without crossing edges in a surface
\Sigma
.
A closed curve
\gamma
in a surface
\Sigma
is
genus-reducing
if the surface (or the two surfaces) obtained from
\Sigma
by cutting along
\gamma
and capping the (up to two) resulting holes has smaller Euler-genus than
\Sigma
.

The
representativity
of the drawing is the minimum number
k
such that every genus-reducing curve that intersects the drawing in vertices only must intersect at least
k
vertices.

Conjecture 15.10

(Wollan
[
Wol22
]
)
.

There exists a polynomial
p
which satisfies the following.
Let
H
be a graph on
t\geq 1
vertices.
Let
\Sigma
be a surface in which
H
can be embedded.
If
G
is a graph drawn in
\Sigma
with representativity at least
p(t)
, then
G
contains
H
as a minor.

In this section we prove a slightly weaker variant of
conjecture

15.9
where we replace “grasped” by “controlled”.
It turns out that this notion is still strong enough to imply
conjecture

15.10
.

Proof of
Theorem

15.1
.

Theorem

15.1
is essentially a further refinement of
theorem

15.8
by using
corollary

5.8
more carefully.

For the sake of slightly better bounds, we look back to the proof of
theorem

15.4
.
Indeed, recall from the proof of
theorem

15.4
that, during the construction phase of the surface, we maintain a
(k+\mathsf{c}t^{12})
-surface wall
D_{i}
that has exactly the number of handles and crosscaps has
\Sigma_{i}
which is the surface we have found in the
i
th step.
In the proof of
theorem

15.4
we discussed that we can never reach
i=t^{2}
because then
corollary

5.8
would imply that we have found our
K_{t}
-minor.
However,
corollary

5.8
actually implies that we can never reach a surface
\Sigma_{i}
where
H
embeds since otherwise we would find an
H
-minor controlled by
D_{i}
.
This observation is enough the prove our claim.
∎

Notice that
theorem

15.1
directly implies our second main theorem, namely
theorem

1.2
.
With this, one major milestone of this paper is achieved.

The proof of
conjecture

15.10
.

The following proof of
conjecture

15.10
was presented to us by Wollan in a discussion of the results of this paper.
He kindly agreed for us to include it here
[
Wol25
]
.

We will prove the following theorem, where the value of
\mathsf{r}(g,t)
is defined as further below.

Theorem 15.11
.

For all positive integers
g
and
t
the following holds:
Every graph that can be drawn with representativity at least
\mathsf{r}(g,t)\in\mathsf{poly}(t+g)
in a surface
\Sigma
of Euler-genus at most
g
contains, as a minor, every graph
H
on at most
t
vertices that can be embedded in
\Sigma
.

Let us give a short outline before we introduce some required definitions and results.
The idea is as follows:
Fix
H
to be a graph on
t
vertices and let
\mathsf{p}(t)
(roughly) be the sum of
\mathsf{mesh}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}}(t,3,t^{2})

\mathsf{apex}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}}(t,t^{2})
and
y(t)\cdot t^{2}\mathsf{depth}_{\ref{thm:strongest_localstructure}}(t,t^{2})
, where the value of
y(t)
will become clear later on.

Let
G
be a graph that embeds on a surface
\Sigma
where
H
minimally embeds such that any embedding of
G
in
\Sigma
has representativity at least
\mathsf{p}(t)
.
Due to the high representatitivty,
G
must have large treewidth and thus, contain a huge mesh which allows for the application of
theorem

15.1
.
Let
\Sigma^{\prime}
be the surface for which
theorem

15.1
returns a
\Sigma^{\prime}
-decomposition
\delta
.

Notice that the Euler-genus of
\Sigma^{\prime}
must be smaller than the Euler-genus of
\Sigma
as otherwise we would have that
H
is a minor of
G
.

Next we may assume that all the non-vortex cells of
\delta
are empty as otherwise we would find a subdivision of
K_{3,3}
or
K_{5}
that can be removed from
G
by deleting a small set of vertices.
This is impossible as this would drop the Euler-genus of
G
by at least
1
.

Finally, we consider any vortex
c_{0}
of
\delta
.
Using a known trick for bounded depth vortices allows us to either find many disjoint crosses arranged in series around the boundary of
c_{0}
, or a small set of vertices (in size bounded by
y(t)
) whose deleting removes all such crosses and allows to fully embed the vortex into a disk.
In the first case, one can find a
K_{3,t^{2}}
as a minor of
G
, certifying that the Euler-genus of
G
is larger than the one of
\Sigma
, a contradiction.
So the second case must hold.
This, however, means that
G
embeds into
\Sigma^{\prime}
after deleting a small set of vertices which is a contradiction to the representativity of
G
on
\Sigma
.

First, we introduce some necessary properties of embeddings of high representativity.

Proposition 15.12

(Demaine, Fomin, Hajiaghayi, and Thilikos
[
DFHT05
]
)
.

Let
k
be a positive integer.
Let
G
be a graph embedded
43
43

43

We mean here explicitly
2
-cell embedded
.
in a surface
\Sigma
not homeomorphic to a sphere, with representativity at least
4k
.
Then
G
contains a
k
-mesh.

We shall need a relation between the Euler-genus of a graph
G
and the Euler-genus of a surface where
G
can be drawn with high representativity.

Proposition 15.13

(Seymour, Thomas
[
ST96
]
)
.

Let
k
be a positive integer.
Let
G
be a
3
-connected graph with a cross-free drawing
\Gamma
of representativity
k
on a surface
\Sigma
and let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be a cross-free drawing of
G
of representativity at least
3
in another surface
\Sigma^{\prime}
.
Suppose
k\geq\{320,5\log\mathsf{eg}(\Sigma)\}
, then either there is a homeomorphism from
\Sigma
to
\Sigma^{\prime}
taking
\Gamma
to
\Gamma^{\prime}
, or
\mathsf{eg}(\Sigma^{\prime})\geq\mathsf{eg}(\Sigma)+10^{-4}k^{2}
.

Notice that
proposition

15.13
requires
3
-connectivity.
To deal with this, we import another result, this time due to Robertson and Vitray.

Let
G
be a
2
-connected graph and let
G_{1},G_{2}\subseteq G
be two edge-disjoint subgraphs of
G
such that
G=G_{1}\cup G_{2}
which intersect in exactly two vertices, namely
x
and
y
.
For both
i\in[2]
let
G_{i}^{\prime}
the the subgraph of
G
obtained from
G_{i}
by adding an
x
-
y
-path
P^{i}_{xy}\subseteq G_{3-i}
.
We call the operation defined above a
2
-split
of
G
.
A subgraph
H
of
G
is a
3
-connected block
44
44

44

There are several ways to define
3
-blocks and the like. We use this definition as it is the one used in
proposition

15.14
.
if it can be obtained from
G
by a sequence of
2
-splits.
Any
3
-connected block of a
2
-connected graph
G
is either a subdivision of a
3
-connected graph, or a graph consisting of three parallel paths sharing their endpoints.
The later are called the
trivial

3
-connected blocks of
G
.

Let
G
be a graph.
A
3
-connected block
of
G
is a
3
-connected block of a block of
G
.

Proposition 15.14

(Robertson, Vitray
[
RV90
]
)
.

Let
G
be a graph with a cross-free drawing
\Gamma
of representativity
k\geq 3
in a surface
\Sigma
.
Then there exists a unique non-trivial
3
-connected block
H
of
G
such that the subdrawing
\Gamma^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma
of
H
is a cross-free drawing of representativity
k
of
H
in
\Sigma
.

Observation 15.15
.

Let
k
be a positive integer,
\Sigma
be a surface that is not the sphere, and
G
be a graph with a cross-free drawing
\Gamma
of representativity
k
in a
\Sigma
.
Then, for every set
S\subseteq V(G)
of at most
k-1
vertices, the drawing
\Gamma^{\prime}\subseteq\Gamma
of
G-S
is of representativity at least
k-|A|\geq 1
.

Combining
proposition

15.13
,
proposition

15.14
, and
observation

15.15
yields the following corollary about deleting small vertex sets from highly representative graphs.

Corollary 15.16
.

Let
G
be a graph and
\Sigma
be a surface of Euler-genus
g\geq 1
.
Let
r\geq 320+5\lceil\log g\rceil+100k+t
and let
\Gamma
be a cross-free drawing of
G
on
\Sigma
with representatitivity
r
.
Then for every set
A\subseteq V(G)
with
|A|\leq t
, and every cross-free drawing
\Gamma^{\prime}
of
